- **Human review:** Confirm that HIR should depend directly on the diagnostics crate for validation output; that attributes containing parser/lexer errors should be skipped after their primary syntax diagnostic; that the `AttributeTarget` context is sufficient and can be derived from the AST/item tree when validation is integrated; and that duplicate operands should compare source spelling, including raw versus escaped string forms.

### IR-103: Build snapshot-aware HIR source maps

- **Decision:** Add a builder scoped to one owner and one parsed file root. Require the root to match its `AstIdMap`, assign owner local ID zero, and verify every node and binding pointer resolves in the same file and syntax-tree snapshot. Store both HIR-node-to-syntax and syntax-to-HIR-node mappings, plus lowering diagnostics. Use standard-library `HashMap` and `Vec` for the reverse map rather than adding `rustc-hash` and `smallvec`.
- **Reason:** Source pointers must not resolve against another file or snapshot, even when the replacement syntax tree has identical text, kinds, and ranges. The existing `SyntaxNodePtr` carries snapshot identity and a tree-local node index, so resolving each pointer against the supplied root provides that guarantee before it enters the map. Multiple HIR nodes can be generated from one syntax node during desugaring, requiring a one-to-many reverse map. The workspace does not currently depend on `FxHashMap` or `SmallVec`; standard collections preserve the planned behavior without new dependencies, and their memory/performance tradeoff can be revisited if profiling warrants it.
- **Review:** The first hostile review identified three issues: public fields could desynchronize the two node maps, diagnostics from another snapshot could be attached to the map, and §2.7.4 incorrectly claimed that the map stores typed `AstPtr`s. The map now has private fields with read-only accessors; `AstIdMap::matches_snapshot` lets the builder verify the root's workspace identity; diagnostics are validated and bound to that snapshot on insertion; and the docs consistently describe heterogeneous `SyntaxNodePtr`s. A second pass found no further code issues but caught that the §2.8.4 struct example still marked the fields public; the example now matches the implementation. The final review confirmed the API documentation matches all five accessors. Regression checks cover foreign-snapshot diagnostics, root/snapshot mismatch, and same-kind/same-range nodes from a different parsed snapshot. The full workspace suite passed all 506 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that standard `HashMap`/`Vec` are acceptable for the reverse map in place of the planned `FxHashMap`/`SmallVec`, and that keeping spans and the `hir_source_map` query for a later lowering milestone is an acceptable boundary.

### IR-104: Define core HIR type structures

- **Decision:** Add typed values for names, lang items, paths, generic arguments and parameters, where predicates, bounds, effects, provenance, and type nodes in `sai_hir::types`. Keep child lists borrowed and records `Copy` to fit the existing copy-only HIR arena contract. Implement semantic equality and hashing for source-bearing records so spans do not affect their values.
- **Reason:** Type lowering and expression/item lowering need one shared representation for types, qualified paths, generic arguments, and bounds. The implementation plan excludes spans from equality used for incremental early cutoff; deriving equality over the full structs would make whitespace-only edits change HIR values. The AST interner already provides stable symbols and an explicit error sentinel, while existing owner and HIR IDs provide the structural identities these records use.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no issues. It verified that each custom equality/hash implementation excludes all span data, including `SpanCtxt`, while retaining HIR IDs and owner identity; it also confirmed the borrowed-slice/`Copy` shape and docs match §2.8.5. Focused HIR tests pass (38 tests), including equality/hash checks across moved spans and examples of error names, generic type paths, const arguments, function bounds, and provenance. The full workspace suite passed all 509 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that source spans should be excluded from equality and hashing for `Name`, `Ty`, `Path`, `GenericArgs`, and `Bound`, including `SpanCtxt`; and that borrowed child slices are the right interface while stable HIR slice allocation remains a separate step.

### IR-105: Allocate stable copyable slices in the HIR arena

- **Decision:** Extend `sai_intern::Arena<T: Copy>` to retain both individual boxed values and boxed slices. Add `alloc_slice(&[T]) -> &[T]`, sharing the arena's mutex and lifetime boundary, and count both kinds of allocation in `len()`.
- **Reason:** HIR nodes borrow child lists, and the type records introduced in IR-104 are copyable. Stable boxed slices let those lists live for the arena lifetime without storing references into a growable vector. Keeping the `Copy` bound preserves the arena's existing teardown guarantee that element destructors cannot observe other arena entries after their lifetimes end.
- **Review:** Focused `sai_intern` tests pass (13 tests), including stable addresses after vector growth, empty slices, and concurrent slice allocation. The full workspace suite passed all 511 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied and formatting passed. The hostile review found no issues in either the existing value allocation after the storage refactor or the new slice allocation; it confirmed the Box pointees remain stable as the storage vectors grow, the returned borrow is tied to the arena, and the `Copy` bound prevents teardown destructors. Miri could not run because the available stable toolchain does not include the `cargo-miri` component.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `Arena::len()` should count both individual values and slices, including empty slices, and that copying a borrowed input slice is the intended allocation API for lowering.

### IR-106: Add typed arenas for HIR type nodes

- **Decision:** Add `sai_hir::arena::HirArena<'hir>` with a typed `Arena<T>` for each type/path/generic/bound/effect record and each corresponding child-list element type. Expose one-value and copied-slice allocation methods that return references tied to the arena lifetime. The query session owns the arena; `HirOwner` borrows its allocations and does not embed the arena.
- **Reason:** The type records borrow child values and lists, so storing them in growable vectors would invalidate references. Typed arenas make the lifetime relationship explicit and build on the copy-only value/slice storage implemented in IR-105 without unsafe code in `sai_hir`. The hostile review confirmed that embedding the arena beside references into it would make `HirOwner` self-referential and unavailable to safe Rust moves. Session ownership follows the existing per-session arena model and keeps storage alive for memoized HIR owner values.
- **Review:** Focused HIR tests pass (39 tests), including nested type paths, generic arguments, bounds, effects, provenance, and reference stability while the arenas grow. The full workspace suite passed all 512 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The hostile review found no allocation or lifetime defects in `HirArena`; it identified the self-reference issue in the previous ownership wording, which this decision and the §2.8.5 text correct. Follow-up review confirmed the ownership wording and human-review note are consistent.
- **Human review:** Confirm that query-session ownership is the right lifetime boundary and that session-wide retention is acceptable for memoized HIR values; also confirm the separate `Arena<T>` stores per node/list element kind before introducing interning or allocation deduplication.

### IR-107: Add literal and pattern HIR nodes

- **Decision:** Define decoded `Lit` values and the complete `Pat`/`PatKind` family, preserving the three distinct slice-rest states and constructor argument presence. Allocate pattern nodes, argument lists, literals, and byte-string payloads in `HirArena`. Store byte strings as `&'hir [u8]` borrowed from the session arena instead of `Arc<[u8]>`.
- **Reason:** Patterns reuse decoded literal, name, path, and type values and need stable HIR IDs for bindings. The nested option for slice rest distinguishes no `..`, bare `..`, and `..` with a subpattern; `Option` around constructor args distinguishes no parentheses from explicit empty parentheses. Borrowing byte-string payloads keeps literal nodes `Copy` and gives their bytes the same arena lifetime as their HIR owner values without another reference-counted allocation.
- **Review:** Focused HIR tests pass (42 tests), covering binding modes, constructor shape/rest, signed literals, all slice-rest states, source-span-insensitive pattern equality/hash, and byte-string payload stability. The full workspace suite passed all 515 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The hostile review confirmed the constructor-argument presence regression test distinguishes omitted from explicitly empty parentheses. It found that three schema references still omitted the `Lit<'hir>` lifetime; the Expr, Pat, and PatRangeEnd references are now aligned. Follow-up review confirmed there are no further findings in the pattern shapes, equality/hash, IDs, arena allocations, or module exports.
- **Human review:** Confirm the borrowed `ByteStr` lifetime and session retention are acceptable in place of `Arc<[u8]>`, and that pattern equality should ignore the entire `Span`, including its desugaring context.

### IR-108: Define expression, block, and statement HIR

- **Decision:** Add the documented expression/control-flow/operator/closure/format-string, block, and statement records. Extend the session-owned `HirArena` with a typed allocator and stable child-list allocator for each node/list kind. Implement semantic equality and hashing for source-bearing records that omits source spans but retains HIR IDs, safety, operation kinds, and synthesized-arm state.
- **Reason:** The specified expression and statement forms are the shared input for later lowering, type checking, and MIR construction. Recursive references and list fields fit the existing `Copy`-only session arena. Keeping source positions out of structural equality follows the incremental query contract, while retaining `HirId` and semantic fields prevents distinct operations or synthesized arms from collapsing.
- **Review:** Focused HIR tests pass (46 tests), covering span-insensitive equality/hash for expressions, blocks, statements, labels, and format specs, plus allocation of nested blocks, statements, matches, closures, and format strings. The full workspace suite passed all 519 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The hostile review found no issues: node shapes match §2.8.5, semantic equality retains HIR IDs and control-flow/safety state while excluding spans, and all new arena fields have matching allocation methods.
- **Human review:** Confirm the expression node inventory matches §2.8.5, especially `ExprKind::Call::base`, `MatchSource`, `LoopSource`, `synthesized`, and unresolved `AsCast`; and confirm source spans should not participate in equality or hashing for these node records.

### IR-109: Define function signatures and body HIR

- **Decision:** Add `FnSig`, `FnParam`, `Body`, `BodyParam`, `Contract`, and `ContractKind` records, and typed arena allocation for their nodes and child lists. Remove the planned lifetime parameter from `BodyParam`, since it contains no borrowed field and Rust rejects an unused lifetime parameter.
- **Reason:** Function signatures link parameters, return types, effects, provenance, and contract HIR IDs; body values own the corresponding expression, body parameters, contracts, and invariant predicates. Storing the records and all reference/ID lists in the session-owned `HirArena` keeps the lifetimes explicit. A lifetime on `BodyParam` would not constrain any field and has no type-safety effect.
- **Review:** Focused HIR tests pass (48 tests), including signature and body construction with contracts and invariant expressions, plus typed-arena allocation for function parameters, body parameters, contract lists, HIR-ID lists, and expression-reference lists. The full workspace suite passed all 521 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The hostile review found no issues in the schema shapes, owner/HIR/binding IDs, lifetimes, Copy behavior, allocator coverage, or the removal of the unused `BodyParam` lifetime.
- **Human review:** Confirm that body contract expressions should be referenced both by `FnSig::{requires, ensures}` HIR IDs and by `Body::contracts`, and that invariant predicates remain separate from the body's `Unit` value as specified.

### IR-110: Define item and owner HIR structures

- **Decision:** Add `HirOwner`, `OwnerNode`, ordinary and associated item records, extern items, interface/world items, fields, variants, and synthetic derived-impl owners. Extend `HirArena` with typed storage and copied child-list allocators for each item family. Use arena-backed `&str` for test/property names and WIT package paths in place of the planned `SmolStr`.
- **Reason:** These item records complete the data model that syntax lowering and later owner-based queries need. The arena stores stable `Copy` nodes, while `HirOwner` borrows the session arena and validated raw attributes and owns its diagnostics. The repository has no `SmolStr` dependency; borrowed UTF-8 strings keep item nodes copyable and reuse the arena's stable byte storage. Equality and hashing omit direct source spans, including the interface refinement clause and package-path span, to preserve structural values across text movement.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found a missing arena allocator for nonempty owner-ID child lists and insufficient regression coverage for source-span-insensitive equality/hash. `HirArena` now stores `OwnerId` slices, and tests cover nonempty lists, moved spans with matching hashes, and changed owner/definition identities across every source-bearing owner record. The follow-up confirmed both findings are resolved and found no remaining actionable issues. Focused HIR tests pass (51 tests); the full workspace suite passed all 524 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that arena-backed `&str` is the right replacement for `SmolStr` in these HIR values; that item identity equality should omit spans while preserving owner, definition, name, visibility, and semantic kind; and that the session arena plus borrowed raw attributes is the right lifetime boundary for `HirOwner`.

### IR-111: Create spans alongside source-map node identities

- **Decision:** Add `HirSourceMapBuilder::{add_node_with_span, add_binding_with_span, span_for}`. Each method validates that its syntax node belongs to the builder's file root before returning a `Span`; node and binding allocation methods also update the source map in the same operation.
- **Reason:** Every HIR node and binding needs both a source location and a mapping to syntax. Building these together prevents lowering from assigning locations from another file or syntax snapshot and preserves an explicit desugaring context without duplicating source-map validation.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed that node and binding methods validate syntax before allocating IDs, node IDs use the source-map insertion path, `span_for` does not allocate IDs, and returned spans preserve the root, range, and requested context. Focused HIR tests pass (52 tests); the full workspace suite passed all 525 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that syntax-node ranges are the correct default span boundaries for each lowered construct and that callers should choose `SpanCtxt` explicitly at each allocation site.

### IR-112: Lower syntax literals to HIR expressions

- **Decision:** Add `sai_hir::lower::lower_literal` for all seven literal families. It interns decoded text and stores byte-string payloads in the HIR arena, removes `_` separators from retained float text, and lowers missing tokens, `ERROR` nodes, and malformed numeric tokens to `ExprKind::Err`. Error-marked string, byte-string, character, and byte tokens retain the recovery values returned by their AST decoders.
- **Reason:** Literal decoding already lives in the syntax AST and HIR has a complete `Lit` representation. Reusing those decoders keeps lexer/parser behavior authoritative, while the existing source-map builder gives each expression its owner-local ID and validated span. Normalizing float separators keeps HIR equality independent of non-semantic digit separators while retaining decimal text for later target-width conversion. Preserving total decoder recovery values avoids losing the specified replacement character or zero when the lexer has already reported an invalid escape or character.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that treating every `HAS_ERROR` token as `Err` discarded required recovery values for strings, byte strings, chars, and bytes. Lowering now distinguishes `ERROR` nodes from token-level errors, retaining those decoder values while still mapping malformed numeric tokens to `Err`; a second hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. Focused tests cover every literal family, decoded and recovered payloads, normalized float text, HIR ID/source-span mapping, and malformed-integer recovery. The full workspace suite passed 528 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed after the correction.
- **Human review:** Confirm that float HIR should retain decimal text without `_` rather than source spelling, and that strings should be interned while byte strings remain arena-backed slices.

### IR-113: Lower literal patterns to HIR

- **Decision:** Add `sai_hir::lower::lower_literal_pat` for integer, string, byte-string, character, byte, and boolean patterns. Preserve a leading minus only through `Lit::Int::negated`; lower float patterns, missing values, error nodes, and malformed numeric tokens to `PatKind::Err`. Retain integer overflow as `value: None` so type checking can report its range error.
- **Reason:** The parser already reports `FLOAT_PATTERN`, while impl/03 owns integer range checking. Reusing the expression literal decoder keeps escape recovery and arena allocation consistent between expression and pattern literals.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed float patterns become `PatKind::Err` after the parser's `FLOAT_PATTERN` diagnostic, while malformed numbers, overflow, negative integer patterns, decoder recovery values, and source-map IDs/spans follow the plan. Focused coverage verifies all supported literal kinds, negative and out-of-range integers, malformed integer recovery, float-pattern recovery, owner-local IDs, and source spans. The full workspace suite passed all 529 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a float pattern should remain `PatKind::Err` after the parser's `FLOAT_PATTERN` diagnostic and that a leading minus is represented only on integer literals.

### IR-114: Lower wildcard and parenthesized patterns

- **Decision:** Extend `sai_hir::lower::lower_pattern` to lower wildcard, literal, and parenthesized patterns. Parentheses are erased by returning the inner pattern directly; malformed/missing contents become `PatKind::Err`. Return `UnsupportedSyntax` for other well-formed patterns until their lowering exists.
- **Reason:** These forms need no semantic resolution or synthesized nodes. Erasing parentheses follows the documented HIR shape and avoids assigning a HIR ID to a syntax-only wrapper. An explicit unsupported result prevents incomplete lowering from silently producing a misleading `Err` node.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed wildcards receive their own HIR ID/span, literals delegate once, parentheses reuse the inner node/span, missing or error-node contents become `PatKind::Err`, and valid unsupported forms return `UnsupportedSyntax` without allocating an HIR node. Tests cover wildcard and nested parenthesized literal patterns, inner-node source spans, owner-local ID ordering, and explicit rejection of tuple patterns. The full workspace suite passed all 530 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that parenthesized patterns should map directly to the inner HIR node and that callers should handle `UnsupportedSyntax` while pattern lowering remains incomplete.

### IR-115: Lower literal range patterns

- **Decision:** Add `sai_hir::lower::lower_range_pat` and route range variants through `lower_pattern`. Support integer, character, byte, and other syntactically valid literal endpoints, including open and inclusive range forms. Invalid endpoints or missing required sides become `PatKind::Err`; const-path endpoints return `UnsupportedSyntax` until path lowering is available.
- **Reason:** Range HIR stores literal endpoints as values inside one `PatKind::Range`, so endpoint literals must not receive independent HIR IDs. The parser owns `FLOAT_PATTERN` and other syntax diagnostics, while the type checker owns semantic range and type checks.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. A local review caught that the first draft did not dispatch range variants through `lower_pattern`; the dispatcher and tests were updated before commit. Focused tests cover inclusive/exclusive ranges, `a..`, `..=b`, negative endpoints, character/byte values, float recovery, const-path rejection without ID allocation, source spans, and owner-local IDs. The full workspace suite passed all 531 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that this implementation should retain syntactically valid but semantically mismatched literal endpoint kinds for type checking and that const-path range ends should wait for path lowering.

### IR-116: Lower binding and `@` patterns

- **Decision:** Lower binding modes, simple bindings, and `name @ pattern` through `lower_pattern`. Assign binding IDs in source pre-order, normalize names to NFC, remove valid raw prefixes, and use `Symbol::ERROR` for missing, reserved, or invalid raw binding names. Expose the syntax crate's reserved-word classifier instead of duplicating its keyword table in HIR. Invalid raw identifiers that parse as constructor paths or range-end paths, such as `r#Self`, lower to `PatKind::Err`.
- **Reason:** The lexer already owns identifier diagnostics, while green syntax tokens do not preserve the separate `RESERVED` flag and an invalid raw uppercase `Self` token is not marked with `HAS_ERROR`. Sharing the lexer’s reserved-word classifier avoids duplicate keyword tables; checking raw path segments lets HIR retain the lexer diagnostic as authoritative instead of misclassifying the pattern as an unfinished valid constructor or range path. Normalizing non-NFC identifiers preserves the documented identity after their lexer diagnostic.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found that `r#Self` was parsed as a constructor and returned `UnsupportedSyntax`, despite its existing `InvalidRawIdent` diagnostic. The follow-up found the same issue for range endpoints such as `r#Self..=4`. Both cases now detect invalid raw path segments and lower to `PatKind::Err`; the final hostile review found no remaining actionable issues and confirmed pre-order IDs, spans, and source-map ordering. Focused tests cover all four binding modes, nested `@` bindings, binding ID pre-order, source-map counts and spans, reserved and raw identifiers (including `r#Self` and `r#Self..=4`), invalid raw binding names, and NFC normalization. The full workspace suite passed all 533 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that invalid raw binding names should use `Symbol::ERROR`, invalid raw pattern paths should become `PatKind::Err`, and an NFC diagnostic should still allow a normalized HIR binding name.

### IR-117: Lower tuple patterns

- **Decision:** Lower `TuplePat` recursively to `PatKind::Tuple`, including the empty tuple pattern `()` and single-element tuple patterns. Assign the tuple's HIR ID before lowering its elements, and preflight all elements so a valid but unsupported nested pattern does not leave partial source-map entries or binding IDs.
- **Reason:** The HIR already defines tuple patterns as an ordered slice of child patterns, and the parser distinguishes tuple syntax from parenthesized patterns. Reserving the parent identity first preserves source pre-order; validating descendants before allocation keeps the explicit `UnsupportedSyntax` contract consistent for nested forms.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. Focused coverage checks unit and singleton tuples, nested tuple/binding modes, source ranges, owner-local and binding ID order, and rejection of a tuple containing an unsupported constructor without partial allocation. The focused lowering suite passed all 8 tests; the full workspace suite passed all 534 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `()` and `(pattern,)` should remain `PatKind::Tuple` nodes and that an unsupported descendant should reject the containing tuple atomically.

### IR-118: Lower slice patterns

- **Decision:** Lower `SlicePat` elements into `PatKind::Slice { before, rest, after }` by comparing each element's source range to the `REST_PAT` range. Preserve a missing rest as `None`, bare `..` as `Some(None)`, and a bound rest as `Some(Some(binding))`; lower a second rest to `PatKind::Err` because the parser reports `MULTIPLE_REST_PATTERNS` but retains both nodes. Preflight ordinary children before allocating the slice.
- **Reason:** Generated AST accessors expose slice elements and rest nodes as separate ordered iterators, while HIR requires their interleaved source order. Source ranges recover that order without changing the syntax API. Reusing binding lowering retains mode, name normalization, spans, and owner-local binding IDs.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. Focused coverage checks empty slices, leading/middle/trailing bare and bound rests, binding modes and ID order, source spans, duplicate-rest recovery, and rejection of a slice containing an unsupported constructor without partial allocation. The focused lowering suite passed all 9 tests; the full workspace suite passed all 535 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm source-range partitioning for before/rest/after and that a parser-diagnosed second rest should make the whole slice `PatKind::Err`.

### IR-119: Lower implicit-member patterns

- **Decision:** Lower `.Variant` and `.Variant(args)` patterns to `PatKind::ImplicitMember`, preserving whether arguments are absent or explicitly empty, the source order of positional and named arguments, and a trailing constructor rest as `has_rest`. Reuse name normalization and recursive pattern lowering; preflight nested patterns before allocating the parent.
- **Reason:** The HIR already has a dedicated implicit-member node because the enum type and variant are resolved later. Generated argument accessors separate positional and named arguments, so lowering walks direct CST children to retain their interleaving. A trailing `..` has no HIR pattern node and is represented by the existing boolean field.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed absent versus empty arguments, named/positional source order, trailing rest representation, shared name lowering, and preflight before allocation. Focused tests cover no argument list, empty arguments, rest-only arguments, interleaved named and positional arguments, nested binding modes and IDs, and rejection of a nested constructor before partial allocation. The focused lowering suite passed all 10 tests; the full workspace suite passed all 536 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that implicit variants should retain unresolved names for type-directed resolution and that absent versus empty argument lists are semantically distinct.

### IR-120: Lower constructor paths and patterns

- **Decision:** Lower constructor patterns to `PatKind::Ctor` with their `ConstPath` represented as `PathRoot::Plain` and one HIR segment for every source `NameRef`. Preserve absent versus explicit arguments, source order for positional and named arguments, and trailing `..` as `has_rest`. Reuse the implicit-member argument collector and recursively preflight supported children before allocating the constructor.
- **Reason:** `ConstPath` grammar is `(LIDENT '.')* UIDENT ('.' UIDENT)*`; the lexer gives `Self` its own reserved token, so it cannot be a segment in this syntax. `PathRoot::SelfType` therefore is not applicable to these constructor paths. HIR `Path` and `PathSegment` carry source-map IDs, so lowering records the complete path and each segment, while names use the existing NFC and raw-identifier normalization.
- **Review:** The hostile review found one stale summary sentence that could imply constructor patterns were still unsupported; the implementation summary now names or-patterns and other unimplemented forms specifically. No code findings were reported. The focused `sai_hir` suite passed all 63 tests, the full workspace suite passed all 537 tests, workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that constructor paths should remain unresolved with `PathRoot::Plain`, that the reserved `Self` token should stay outside `ConstPath`, and that explicit empty argument lists remain distinguishable from omitted parentheses.

### IR-121: Lower constant-path range endpoints

- **Decision:** Lower each `ConstPath` range endpoint to `PatRangeEnd::Path` through the shared path lowerer. Pre-decode both endpoints and validate the range shape before allocating the range HIR node or any path/segment source-map IDs; then allocate the range node before its endpoint paths.
- **Reason:** HIR stores endpoints by value, while every path and segment has its own source-map ID. Validating first prevents an invalid literal paired with a valid path from leaving unreachable path IDs in the source map. Reusing the constructor path lowerer keeps segment naming, NFC normalization, roots, spans, and source mappings consistent.
- **Review:** The hostile subagent found no actionable issues and confirmed endpoint/range-shape validation occurs before range or path IDs are allocated, valid paths use parent-before-child IDs, and the documentation matches the implementation. Focused range tests and all 63 `sai_hir` tests passed; the full workspace suite passed all 537 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that constant paths in range patterns should retain unresolved paths for later constant resolution, and that invalid ranges should not allocate endpoint path nodes.

### IR-122: Lower or-patterns and diagnose pattern bindings

- **Decision:** Lower `OrPat` to `PatKind::Or`, flatten nested and parenthesized alternatives, discard a leading `|`, and erase pattern parentheses. Compare normalized binding names and modes against the first alternative and report one `OR_PATTERN_BINDING_MISMATCH` at the `OR_PAT`. Reuse `BindingId`s for the same name across alternatives, report each duplicate at its later occurrence, and map shared IDs to their first actual source occurrence. Keep type compatibility for impl/03.
- **Reason:** Pattern alternatives must expose one logical binding to later HIR consumers while preserving the parser's source structure and diagnostics. A binding-name union is also retained for recovery so a name present only in a later mismatching alternative can still be compared with sibling patterns; alternatives themselves are not treated as duplicate scopes. Duplicate occurrences that would otherwise alias the same logical ID receive a distinct recovery ID, and source-map aliases keep all occurrences of a shared binding consistent.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that a name present only in a later alternative was omitted from sibling duplicate checks and that the initial diagnostic wording implied a mode mismatch even when the name was absent. The planner now preserves the first alternative separately for mismatch checks, accumulates later names for recovery and subsequent ID sharing, and uses wording that covers both missing names and mode differences. Regression tests cover valid reordered alternatives, mode and name mismatches, duplicate locations, nested alias groups, leading pipes, flattened alternatives, later-only names, sibling duplicate detection, and canonical source mappings. The follow-up hostile review confirmed the fix and found no additional actionable issues. The full workspace suite passed all 540 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a mismatch should be reported at the whole `OR_PAT` and identify the first differing name in first-alternative order; that names absent from the first alternative should receive a recovery ID at their first later occurrence; and that duplicate recovery occurrences should use distinct IDs while valid alternatives share one canonical source mapping.

### IR-123: Lower unit, parenthesized, and tuple expressions

- **Decision:** Add `sai_hir::lower::lower_expr` for literals, unit, parenthesized, and tuple expressions. Lower unit to `ExprKind::Tuple([])`, erase parenthesized expressions by returning their contents, and allocate each tuple before its ordered elements. Recursively preflight tuple descendants so valid unsupported forms return `UnsupportedSyntax` without partial HIR or source-map entries; error-marked subtrees become one `ExprKind::Err`.
- **Reason:** HIR has no separate unit-expression variant, and `ExprKind::Tuple` naturally represents both empty and ordered tuples. Parentheses are syntax-only wrappers in the implementation plan. Preflight preserves the existing lowering contract that valid unfinished descendants do not leave partial parent state.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed unit and tuple shapes, parenthesis span/source-map reuse, parent-before-child IDs, and atomic rejection of valid unsupported children. Focused `sai_hir` tests and the full workspace suite passed all 542 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that unit expressions should use an empty `ExprKind::Tuple`, parenthesized expressions should reuse the inner HIR ID and span, and a tuple containing valid unsupported syntax should be rejected before any of its nodes are allocated.

### IR-124: Lower simple path and implicit-member expressions

- **Decision:** Extend `lower_expr` for a path without generic arguments, `self`, `Self`, and `.Variant`. A plain path has one segment; `self` and `Self` use empty `PathRoot::SelfValue` and `PathRoot::SelfType` paths; an implicit member becomes `ExprKind::ImplicitMember`. Give a plain path's expression, path, and segment separate HIR IDs and source mappings. Keep generic path arguments and field access behind `UnsupportedSyntax`.
- **Reason:** The HIR path shape deliberately leaves names unresolved, with distinct roots for receiver value and implementing type. Reusing `lower_name_ref` preserves NFC normalization, raw-identifier handling, and source spans; deferring generic arguments avoids partially representing type/const arguments before type lowering is available.
- **Review:** The hostile subagent found no actionable issues. It confirmed all path roots, separate expression/path/segment IDs and mappings, source spans, and preflight rejection of generic arguments and field access. Focused `sai_hir` tests and the full workspace suite passed all 544 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that bare `self` and `Self` should be empty rooted paths, that `.Variant` should remain an implicit member for expected-type resolution, and that generic arguments should stay unsupported until their type and const argument forms can be lowered.

### IR-125: Lower field and tuple-index expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `FieldExpr` recursively to `ExprKind::Field { args: None }` and `TupleIndexExpr` to `ExprKind::TupleField`, allocating the postfix node before lowering its base. Preserve the AST tuple-index decoder's `Option<u32>` as the HIR index, including `None` after parser-diagnosed spellings or a value outside `u32`. Reject field generic arguments before allocation until generic-argument lowering is implemented.
- **Reason:** Nested fields preserve the unresolved left-to-right expression shape required by the resolver (`a.b.c` is `Field(Field(Path(a), b), c)`). The syntax parser already diagnoses invalid tuple-index spellings, while the HIR's optional index preserves an undecodable value without inventing another diagnostic. Preflighting field bases keeps valid unsupported generic arguments from leaving partial source-map entries.
- **Review:** The hostile subagent found no actionable issues. It confirmed nested field shapes, parent-before-base IDs, per-node source mappings, generic-field preflight, and `None` recovery for invalid or out-of-range tuple indexes without duplicate diagnostics. Focused `sai_hir` tests and the full workspace suite passed all 545 tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a tuple index that does not decode to `u32` should be represented as `None`, relying on any existing parser diagnostic, and that generic arguments after a field should remain unsupported until their HIR argument values can be represented.

### IR-126: Lower prefix and binary expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `BinExpr` to `ExprKind::Binary`, mapping every syntax operator explicitly, including parser-diagnosed `&&` and `||` recovery tokens to `And` and `Or`. Lower `PrefixExpr` to `Unary` or `Borrow`; fold only a direct integer or float literal under unary minus into one negated `Lit` mapped to the full prefix span. Keep other uses of unary minus as `Unary(Neg, …)`. Preflight both binary operands and prefix operands before allocating their HIR parents.
- **Reason:** The HIR already distinguishes unary operators from borrow mutability, and the docs specify that negative numeric literals represent one constant while all other negative expressions keep a unary operation. Mapping `&&` and `||` to the corresponding boolean operators preserves the recoverable syntax tree while the parser's `USE_AND_OR` diagnostic remains authoritative. Parent-first IDs and preflight preserve the established source-map and atomic-lowering behavior.
- **Review:** Focused lowering tests cover every binary and prefix operator, both parser-diagnosed boolean spellings, negative integer/float folding and source spans, unary fallback, borrow mutability, parent-first IDs, and rejection of unsupported descendants before allocation. The hostile subagent found no actionable issues and confirmed these mappings and recovery paths match the documentation. All 548 workspace tests passed; Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm the recovery policy for `&&` / `||`, that negative numeric literals should map to the full prefix range as one HIR node, and that `borrow mut` should use `Mutability::Mut` while `borrow` uses `Mutability::Not`.

### IR-127: Lower range expressions

- **Decision:** Lower every `RangeExpr` to `ExprKind::Range`, preserving optional endpoints and deriving `inclusive` from its direct `..` or `..=` token. Lower the start before the end and the range parent before either endpoint. Preflight supported endpoints before allocating the range. Keep chained ranges in the nested HIR shape produced by the parser; lower an error-marked range to `ExprKind::Err`.
- **Reason:** The HIR range node directly represents all six range forms, including `..`, without desugaring or synthetic nodes. Reading the operator from the range's direct tokens distinguishes inclusive forms independently of endpoint presence. The parser already owns missing-endpoint and chained-range diagnostics, so HIR recovery should preserve usable syntax and avoid duplicate diagnostics.
- **Review:** Focused lowering tests cover all six forms, endpoint order and source mappings, unsupported generic-path endpoints rejected before allocation, missing inclusive endpoints recovering as `Err`, and parser-diagnosed chained ranges retaining their nested HIR shape. The hostile subagent found no actionable issues and confirmed operator detection, recovery, endpoint order, and preflight. All 551 workspace tests passed; Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a missing endpoint after `..=` should lower to `Err` when the parser marks its recovery subtree, and that chained ranges should preserve their nested structure while relying on the parser diagnostic.

### IR-128: Lower index, try, and await expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `IndexExpr`, `TryExpr`, and `AwaitExpr` to their matching HIR variants. Assign each postfix parent's HIR ID before its operands; for indexing, lower the base before the index. Preflight every operand before allocating the expression, and lower malformed error-marked postfix syntax as one `ExprKind::Err`.
- **Reason:** These postfix forms have dedicated HIR nodes because indexing, `?`, and `.await` keep distinct typing and evaluation semantics. Retaining the parser's left-associated nesting preserves expressions such as `nested[a][b]?`. Stage gating and semantic checks for `await` belong to later analysis; syntax lowering only preserves its dedicated HIR form.
- **Review:** Focused lowering tests cover simple indexing, nested left-associated indexing, postfix `?`, `.await`, parent-first IDs, rejection of an unsupported generic path before allocation, and malformed index recovery without duplicate diagnostics. The hostile subagent found no actionable issues and confirmed operand order, association, recovery, and Stage 5 deferral. All 553 workspace tests passed; Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `.await` should remain `ExprKind::Await` at syntax lowering, with Stage 5 eligibility checked later, and that nested postfix forms should preserve their parsed left association.

### IR-129: Lower calls and call arguments

- **Decision:** Lower `CallExpr` to `ExprKind::Call`, collecting positional, named, and base arguments by walking direct `ArgList` children so their written order is retained. Store at most one `..base` in the dedicated base field; preserve parent-first IDs and lower the callee and argument expressions in source order. A recoverable missing argument expression becomes an `Err` in that argument's slot, keeping valid siblings. Ignore error wrappers containing invalid argument attributes because the expression still forms an argument. Reject generic path/field callees before allocation until generic-argument lowering is implemented. An absent callee/argument list or multiple base arguments becomes `ExprKind::Err`.
- **Reason:** The HIR stores call arguments in an ordered slice, while the typed AST exposes positional, named, and base arguments as separate role iterators. Walking direct children preserves interleaving. The parser omits an empty positional argument node but retains a zero-width `ERROR`; inserting an `Err` placeholder preserves argument order and prevents the call from silently losing valid siblings. Attribute-target errors wrap only the attribute and must not add a spurious argument. The HIR has only one base slot, so multiple base arguments cannot be represented faithfully and must recover as `Err`; parser diagnostics remain authoritative. Generic callees need the later type/const generic-argument lowering and should not leave partial call nodes.
- **Review:** Hostile review found three recovery issues: recursive error detection discarded the whole call for `call(, 1)`; an attribute-only error before a named or base argument could be mistaken for that argument's missing expression; and nested call recovery could hide a separate missing tuple element. Lowering now preserves the call and tuple, maps each missing slot to its own zero-width `ERROR`, keeps valid siblings, and ignores attribute-only wrappers when collecting call and tuple entries. Regression coverage verifies source mappings for positional, named, and base errors and confirms unsupported valid siblings still fail preflight before allocation. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 82 HIR tests and all 556 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm the single-base recovery policy given the HIR's `Option` field, that call generic arguments should remain unsupported until type and const argument lowering is available, and that a missing argument should remain an `Err` argument while the surrounding call is preserved.

### IR-130: Lower array expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `ArrayExpr` to `ExprKind::Array`, retaining element order and assigning the array HIR ID before its elements. Preflight valid elements before allocating the array. Walk direct syntax children so a direct `ERROR` node remains an `ExprKind::Err` element whenever recovery preserves the surrounding list; ignore attribute-only error wrappers.
- **Reason:** The HIR array is an ordered expression slice, while typed AST iteration omits parser recovery nodes. Call-argument recovery can require lowering an enclosing expression despite another error elsewhere in its subtree, so retaining direct array errors prevents a missing element from disappearing when that recovery path is active. Source-order traversal also keeps nested array IDs and source mappings deterministic.
- **Review:** Hostile review found four preflight gaps. A multiply-based malformed nested call could make call collection fail early, skipping a later valid unsupported argument; preflight now walks direct positional, named, and base expressions independently of argument-list validity. A nested array with its own missing element could skip preflight when an outer sibling triggered call recovery, and an attribute-error wrapper inside parentheses could hide another unsupported child. Finally, error-marked field syntax with generic arguments fell through error recovery even though field lowering returns `UnsupportedSyntax`. Preflight now recursively checks visible operands of supported expressions and explicitly rejects generic path/field forms even when they contain unrelated recovery errors; list lowering retains direct `ERROR` nodes as `Err` elements. Regression tests cover these cases and confirm `UnsupportedSyntax` occurs before array IDs are allocated. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 84 HIR tests and all 558 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a missing array element should remain an `Err` list entry when the surrounding array is retained for local recovery, and that unsupported valid elements should reject the whole array before allocating nodes.

### IR-131: Lower return, break, and continue expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `ReturnExpr`, `BreakExpr`, and `ContinueExpr` to their matching HIR variants. Preserve optional return/break values and optional break/continue labels. Allocate the transfer expression before its value, normalize the identifier after the label's leading apostrophe, and span the label over its complete token. Preflight present values before allocating the transfer expression.
- **Reason:** These syntax forms already have direct HIR representations and require no control-flow resolution at this stage. The lexer supplies a single `LABEL` token, so stripping its quote and using the shared NFC normalizer yields the same symbol identity rules as other identifiers. The parent `span_for` validation ensures label locations belong to the active source tree; label target lookup and function-boundary checks remain later semantic work.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that a malformed explicit `return` or `break` value could be mistaken for an absent value when the parser retained it as a direct `ERROR` child. Optional-value lowering now preserves that distinction as `Some(Err)`; regression coverage checks it alongside absent/present values and labels, interned label identity, full-token label spans, parent-first IDs, and preflight rejection before parent allocation. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 85 HIR tests and all 559 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that label spans should include the leading apostrophe and that label names should use normalized identifier identity while preserving parser-owned syntax diagnostics.

### IR-132: Lower expression blocks and expression statements

- **Decision:** Lower ordinary block expressions with expression statements. Earlier expression statements become `StmtKind::Expr`; the final expression statement becomes `Block::tail`. Retain each direct block `ERROR`, including attribute-wrapped recovery nodes, as an `Err` expression statement. Empty blocks are safe and have no tail. Reject other statement kinds before allocating HIR nodes.
- **Reason:** This implements the block and tail shape already specified by HIR while keeping statement forms that need type, pattern, or assignment lowering for later increments. Preserving all direct `ERROR` children prevents parser recovery from deleting invalid statements or turning an earlier expression into the block tail. The expression and block receive IDs before statement and expression children, following HIR pre-order.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that filtering attribute-wrapped `ERROR` nodes could drop an invalid item and incorrectly promote the preceding call to the block tail. The block collector now preserves those nodes and a regression test checks the resulting statement, tail, and source-map entries. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 87 HIR tests and all 561 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that direct block `ERROR` nodes should be represented as `StmtKind::Expr(Err)` and should prevent an earlier expression statement from becoming the block tail. Other statement kinds remain an intentional incremental boundary.

### IR-133: Lower plain `if` expressions

- **Decision:** Lower ordinary expression conditions and their `then`, `else`, and recursive `else if` branches to `ExprKind::If`. The `then` child is a HIR block; an `else` block is represented by a block expression. Reject `if let` conditions before allocating HIR nodes.
- **Reason:** The plain-if shape already exists in HIR and the implementation plan explicitly keeps plain `if` as `ExprKind::If`; `if let` requires the separate `MatchSource::IfLet` desugaring. Mapping the else-block expression and its HIR block to the same source block preserves source spans without inventing source syntax.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that malformed but otherwise supported binary/index expressions could skip preflight of a present child when a sibling was missing. Preflight now visits each visible child independently for binary, prefix, range, and index expressions. Regression coverage confirms a generic field under a call in a malformed binary and a missing index still reject before allocating the enclosing `if`. The initial binary sample was parsed as comparisons because its generic arguments had no valid follower; the test now asserts the actual AST shape. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 89 HIR tests and all 563 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the HIR block-expression wrapper for an `else` block may share the block node's syntax pointer and span, and that if-let remains unsupported until Match desugaring is implemented.

### IR-134: Lower match expressions

- **Decision:** Lower a normal match to `ExprKind::Match { source: Normal }`, preserving the scrutinee and arm order. Allocate each match and arm before its children, then lower each pattern, optional guard, and body in source order. Preserve direct arm-list `ERROR` nodes as error arms and missing arm components as `Err`; reject unsupported valid descendants before allocating the match.
- **Reason:** The HIR already has a normal-match node and arm structure, and the parser keeps error nodes outside typed arm iterators when it cannot form a complete arm. Turning those recovery nodes into `Err` pattern/body placeholders retains malformed source and later valid arms without inventing valid pattern syntax. If both the scrutinee and arm list are missing, the first non-attribute direct `ERROR` is the scrutinee and is excluded from the arm list, so one syntax error does not acquire two unrelated roles.
- **Review:** Regression tests cover guarded and unguarded arms, patterns, source-order HIR IDs, malformed scrutinee/arm recovery, separate missing-scrutinee and missing-arm errors, and preflight rejection before parent allocation. Hostile review found two recovery bugs: the missing-scrutinee error could be duplicated as an arm error, and a missing body could select the preceding missing-arrow error when both occupied the same source position. Error selection now uses the same non-attribute rule for the scrutinee, and the final direct error for a missing arm body. Regressions verify source-map ownership for both cases. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 93 HIR tests and 567 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that each direct arm-list `ERROR` should be represented as an `Arm` with `Err` pattern and body, and that when scrutinee and arm-list syntax are both absent the first non-attribute direct error belongs to the scrutinee.

### IR-135: Lower `if let`

- **Decision:** Desugar `if let PAT = EXPR THEN (else ELSE)?` to `ExprKind::Match { source: IfLet }` with a source-backed user arm and a synthesized wildcard fallback. The fallback is an empty tuple when `else` is absent; block and recursive `else if` branches lower from their own syntax. Assign desugared spans over the complete `if let` to the generated match, arm wrappers, wildcard pattern, and empty-tuple fallback. Preflight the pattern, scrutinee, and all branches before allocating any HIR nodes.
- **Reason:** This follows the specified §2.9.4 shape and preserves `MatchSource::IfLet` for later temporary and diagnostic rules. Reusing ordinary pattern, block, and conditional lowering preserves their names, bindings, and source spans; only nodes introduced by the desugaring receive synthetic source context. A no-else fallback must be an empty tuple because that is the specified unit value in this HIR.
- **Review:** Regression tests cover the synthesized fallback and source context, parent-before-child IDs, recursive `else if let`, a binding pattern under call-argument recovery, and rejection of an unsupported scrutinee before allocation. The hostile review found no actionable issues. All 96 HIR tests and 570 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that generated arm and fallback nodes should map to the full `if let` syntax with `SpanCtxt::Desugared(IfLet)`, and that the specified no-else fallback is represented by `ExprKind::Tuple([])`.

### IR-136: Lower syntax types

- **Decision:** Lower path and `Self` types, borrow types, tuples and unit, inference, safe/unsafe function pointers, and mixed type/constant/associated-type generic arguments. Erase `owned` and parentheses. Keep generic arguments in source order. Preserve omitted function-pointer return/effects as `None`, and explicit empty effects as `Some([])`. Lower malformed tuple and generic slots to positional `TyKind::Err` placeholders, but do not synthesize children for missing-close markers. Count extra comma slots only when they are not owned by an enclosing comma-delimited syntax node. A malformed constant literal becomes a type-shaped `Err`; a valid integer outside `u128` remains `ConstArg::Int { value: None }`. Since HIR has no error-effect variant, a missing or malformed explicit effects set makes the whole function-pointer type `Err`. Preflight nested types before allocating HIR nodes.
- **Reason:** These forms have direct representations in the HIR schema, and source-order traversal makes IDs and mappings deterministic. Parentheses and `owned` do not change the represented type. The `Option` fields preserve the distinction between omitted syntax and explicit empty syntax. Positional error placeholders retain later valid arguments without duplicating parser diagnostics. Whole-type recovery is the only faithful representation for malformed effects because no effect-list entry can carry `Err`.
- **Review:** Tests cover nested borrow/tuple/owned and `Self` paths, mixed generic arguments, function-pointer safety/return/effects, malformed constants and effect sets, source maps, and missing-delimiter recovery. Hostile review found and fixed malformed explicit effects clauses being silently dropped; terminal closing-delimiter errors being mistaken for type arguments; repeated commas outside a list losing empty slots; and commas consumed by an enclosing generic list, function-pointer parameter list, enum tuple/record shape, or where clause being misattributed to the inner generic list. Regressions exercise tight and spaced comma runs and each enclosing-list case. The final boundary-owner review found no remaining comma misclassification. Synthesized extra error slots map to the parser's zero-width recovery node. All 109 HIR tests and the full workspace suite passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that omitted function-pointer return/effects mean unit/pure, explicit empty effects remain distinguishable, malformed effects invalidate the whole function-pointer type, malformed constant arguments use `GenericArg::Type(TyErr)`, and syntactically valid overflowing constants are preserved for later range diagnostics.

### IR-137: Lower `let` and `var` statements

- **Decision:** Lower `let PAT (: TYPE)? = EXPR (else BLOCK)?` to `StmtKind::Let`, retaining the pattern, optional type, initializer, and let-else block. Lower `var NAME (: TYPE)? = EXPR` to the same HIR statement with a synthesized `BindingMode::Var` pattern. Keep statement source spans rooted in the source; give the synthesized `var` pattern and its name `SpanCtxt::Desugared(VarStmt)`. A written but missing type remains `Some(TyKind::Err)`, and a missing initializer becomes `ExprKind::Err`. Preserve the outer expression when every parser `ERROR` is nested in a let/var statement so those fields can retain their recovery nodes. Preflight patterns, types, initializers, and let-else blocks before block HIR allocation.
- **Reason:** The statement HIR already has the exact shape for both declarations, and preserving let-else as a block is required for later name resolution and MIR control flow. Reusing normal pattern/type/expression lowering keeps binding IDs and validation consistent. The optional type must distinguish no annotation from an erroneous written annotation. Let/var recovery is localized to fields with existing parser diagnostics; retaining those statements exposes the valid parts of the block while avoiding duplicate diagnostics. `var` introduces a pattern that has no corresponding pattern syntax node, so its pattern span covers the source statement while the binding name keeps its identifier range under the `VarStmt` diagnostic context.
- **Review:** Regression tests cover let patterns and annotations, var binding mode and desugared spans/source-map pointers, let-else, missing type and initializer recovery, call-argument recovery inside an initializer, and preflight rejection of an unsupported initializer before allocation. The hostile subagent review found no actionable issues. All 113 HIR tests and all 587 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm the synthetic `var` pattern/source ranges and desugared contexts; that an explicit annotation with a missing type remains `Some(TyKind::Err)`; and that a recoverable error within a let/var statement should preserve its containing expression and block.

### IR-138: Lower assignment and assert statements

- **Decision:** Lower `=` to `StmtKind::Assign` and every compound assignment operator to its corresponding `StmtKind::AssignOp` operator. Lower `assert COND` and `assert COND, MSG` to `StmtKind::Assert`; capture `cond_text` from the validated decoded source slice for the condition and trim only its surrounding whitespace. When a condition is missing, use an empty interned condition string because the existing parser error is authoritative. Extend preflight to all assignment operands and assert expressions, and preserve blocks whose errors are fully owned by implemented block statements or direct block error nodes. Add `HirSourceMapBuilder::text_for_syntax` so source text is read only after file/root validation.
- **Reason:** Assignments and assertions already have direct HIR forms, and compound assignments must remain dedicated nodes so MIR can evaluate their place only once. `cond_text` supplies the default assertion message and must preserve internal spelling, comments, and whitespace; a reconstructed or normalized expression would change the message. Missing assertion conditions are already diagnosed, so an empty `cond_text` avoids inventing text or another diagnostic. Statement-local recovery retains unaffected statements and block tails, while preflight keeps unsupported valid expressions from leaving partial HIR/source-map allocations. Source-map text access ensures the assertion spelling comes from the exact snapshot associated with its HIR.
- **Review:** Regression tests cover simple assignment, every compound-assignment operator, operand source order, assertions with and without a message, exact condition text including an internal comment, missing condition/message recovery, direct block-error recovery, and rejection of an unsupported assignment operand before allocation. The hostile subagent review found no actionable issues. All 118 HIR tests and all 592 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that condition comments and internal whitespace belong in the default `assert` message, that only surrounding whitespace is trimmed, and that an absent condition should use an empty `cond_text` after parser diagnosis.

### IR-139: Lower loop and while expressions

- **Decision:** Lower a plain `loop` directly to `ExprKind::Loop`, retaining its label, body, and `LoopSource::Loop`. Desugar `while` into a synthetic `Loop` with an `If` tail and an else block that breaks the loop. Desugar `while let` into a synthetic `Loop` with a `MatchSource::WhileLet` tail, a source-backed user arm, and a synthesized wildcard arm that breaks the loop. Map every node introduced by either desugaring to the complete while expression with the corresponding `SpanCtxt`; preserve root spans for source conditions, patterns, and user blocks. Preflight all supported descendants before allocating any HIR nodes.
- **Reason:** These are Stage 1 constructs with exact HIR shapes already defined in §2.9.2–§2.9.3. The existing `Loop`, `If`, and `Match` nodes express the specified control flow without extending the HIR schema. An unlabeled synthesized `break` naturally targets the generated innermost loop, while source labels remain attached to that loop for ordinary later resolution. Separate `WhileLoop` and `WhileLet` contexts keep generated spans and diagnostic wording distinguishable.
- **Review:** Tests cover labeled plain loops and while loops, ordinary and let conditions, `LoopSource`/`MatchSource`, source versus desugared spans, pre-order HIR IDs, source-map entries for every synthesized node, and rejection of a nested unsupported `for` before allocating HIR. Hostile subagent review found no actionable issues. All 121 HIR tests and all 595 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a source `while` should use the documented loop desugaring, that the synthesized unlabeled break should target the generated loop, and that every generated node should map to the full while construct while user-written condition/pattern/body nodes keep root spans.

### IR-140: Preserve cast recovery in HIR

- **Decision:** Lower `CAST_EXPR` to `ExprKind::AsCast` even though casts are invalid SAI syntax, retaining the operand and target type for the type checker's `NO_AS_CAST` diagnostic. A missing operand becomes `ExprKind::Err`; a missing target type becomes `TyKind::Err` at the parser's recovery node. Keep the outer expression when all parser error nodes are contained by cast expressions, and preflight both operands and target types before allocating HIR.
- **Reason:** The normative lowering rules explicitly exempt `CAST_EXPR` from ordinary `ERROR` collapse because only the type checker knows which conversion suggestion is appropriate. An error marked on the cast must not discard a valid operand or prevent its parent expression from retaining the cast node. The recovery exception is limited to errors owned by casts so unrelated malformed siblings continue through established error handling.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that a root-only recovery exception discarded `AsCast` when `value as + 1` nested it in a binary expression; the next pass found that searching beyond the lowered syntax root could mistake an enclosing cast for ownership of an unrelated error. Recovery now preserves a parent expression only when every error is contained by a cast within the supplied syntax subtree. Regression tests cover nested cast recovery, target type and error-span preservation, bounded error ownership, and preflight rejection of a generic call operand before allocation. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 125 HIR tests and all 599 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a cast with a missing target type should remain `AsCast` so `NO_AS_CAST` can still use the operand type, and that the recovery exception should apply only when the syntax errors are contained within casts.

### IR-141: Lower unsafe block expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `UnsafeBlockExpr` to `ExprKind::Block`, setting only the corresponding body block's `safety` to `Safety::Unsafe`. Preserve root source spans for the expression wrapper and body, and preflight the body before allocating either node.
- **Reason:** The HIR already represents unsafe context on `Block`, so no new node or desugaring is needed. Keeping ordinary blocks on the safe lowering path makes the lexical unsafe boundary explicit for later analyses, which identify the unsafe context from the marked block.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover the unsafe marker, source spans, parent-first HIR IDs, and rejection of an unsupported generic field before allocation. The hostile review found no actionable findings. All 127 HIR tests and all 601 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `UnsafeBlockExpr` should reuse `ExprKind::Block` and mark only its body `Block` unsafe, while nested source blocks retain their own syntax-derived safety.

### IR-142: Lower region expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `RegionExpr` to `ExprKind::Region`, normalize its source name, register a `BindingId` at the name syntax, and lower the source body as an ordinary safe `Block`. A missing required name or body recovers as `ExprKind::Err`. Preflight body expressions before allocating HIR nodes or bindings.
- **Reason:** HIR has a dedicated region node and binding identity, preserving the source boundary for type checking and MIR construction without introducing the hidden arena operations during syntax lowering. Shared name lowering keeps Unicode normalization and raw-identifier rules consistent with other bindings.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover the HIR node, normalized name and source span, exact binding-to-name syntax mapping, block span and safety, parent-first HIR IDs, and rejection of an unsupported generic field before allocation. The hostile review found no actionable findings. All 129 HIR tests and all 603 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the binder's `BindingId` should point to the `Name` syntax and that arena construction/lifetime behavior should remain downstream of source-level HIR lowering.

### IR-143: Lower `for` loops

- **Decision:** Desugar regular `ForExpr` to the documented `ForLoop` `Match`/`Loop` iterator protocol using `lang::IntoIteratorIntoIter`, `lang::IteratorNext`, and `OptionSome`/`OptionNone`. Create the synthetic mutable `%iter` binding with a `BindingId` mapped to the source `ForExpr`; retain source labels, iterable, pattern, and body with root spans, and mark all generated nodes `SpanCtxt::Desugared(ForLoop)`. Keep only the `OptionNone` fallback arm synthesized. Leave `for await` unsupported until Stage 5. Preflight the pattern, iterable, and body before allocating nodes or bindings.
- **Reason:** The HIR schema and §2.9.5 already define this iterator protocol, including how the outer match keeps iterable temporaries alive for the full loop. Lang-item paths let type checking resolve core iteration methods without source-level names. Keeping the fallback arm marked synthetic prevents diagnostics that apply only to user-written arms.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover both lang-item calls, mutable iterator borrowing, source and synthesized patterns/arms, label and source spans, desugaring contexts, source-map binding identity, and preflight rejection before allocation. The first hostile review found that the generated `into_iter` call and lang-item path used the whole loop span; these nodes now use the iterable expression's range, with regression assertions for the call, callee, and path. The follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. All 132 HIR tests and all 606 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that only `OptionNone` should set `Arm::synthesized`, that the generated `%iter` binding should map to the `ForExpr` syntax, and that `for await` remains deferred to Stage 5.

### IR-144: Lower format strings

- **Decision:** Lower `FStringExpr` to `ExprKind::FormatString`, using the AST piece view for decoded text and brace escapes, recursively lowering interpolation expressions, and retaining optional format specs. NFC-normalize and intern specifier names; map each specifier span to its source token with `SpanCtxt::Root`. Preflight every interpolation before allocating HIR. Preserve malformed text through the AST decoder's replacement value.
- **Reason:** Format strings are kept in HIR so impl/03 can type-check each argument against `Format` and impl/04 can expand the call sequence. The syntax AST already provides decoded and coalesced text pieces and recovery-aware format-spec accessors, so HIR lowering can preserve those canonical values without duplicating lexer logic. Source specs are not desugared nodes and keep source context.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover decoded and escaped text, ordered interpolation expressions, format-spec names/arguments/token spans, malformed text recovery, and preflight rejection before allocation. The first hostile review found that an `ERROR` node inside an interpolation collapsed the entire format string; lowering now localizes that error to the affected argument, and a regression test confirms sibling text and interpolations survive. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. All 136 HIR tests and all 610 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that specifier names should follow the same NFC normalization as other identifiers, and that a format specifier's span should cover its source token while the expression remains source-level HIR.

### IR-145: Intern lexical child definitions

- **Decision:** Add `DefPathInterner::intern_nested` for `Closure` and `AsyncBlock` components only. Require the caller to supply the owner-local preorder disambiguator and append the component to the lexical parent's path.
- **Reason:** Closure and async-block identities are nested definitions, not owners. Keeping path construction in the interner preserves the parent's package and table identity; restricting this helper to symbol-free components prevents it from bypassing session validation for named definitions.
- **Review:** Tests cover lexical parent paths, nested closure components, async-block components, stable disambiguators, idempotent interning, and rejection of named components. Hostile review found no actionable issues. The focused HIR test and all 137 HIR tests passed, as did all 611 workspace tests, workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check`.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the lowering layer, which can scan the full owner syntax, should assign the shared preorder disambiguator for both closures and async blocks.

### IR-146: Lower closure expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `ClosureExpr` to `ExprKind::Closure`, including source-order parameters, optional types and return type, `var` mutability, `move` capture, and the safe source body. Give every parameter a `BindingId` mapped to its `Name` syntax, including `_`, whose HIR name remains `None`. Recover parser errors in the parameter/list while preserving errors that have their own statement, argument, interpolation, or cast recovery; recover missing names as `Symbol::ERROR` and retain later parameters after separator recovery. Other unhandled closure errors use ordinary error lowering. Require `HirSourceMapBuilder` to receive the `DefPathInterner` that interned its owner. Assign closure and async-block `DefId`s together in shared owner-local preorder, independent of lexical parent, and nest each path under its nearest lexical closure or async-block parent; skip syntax beneath a different item owner. Async-block expression lowering itself remains deferred to Stage 5. Preflight the closure's types and body before allocating HIR nodes or bindings. Mark the public lowering and source-map error enums non-exhaustive. Keep the development workspace at 0.1.0; the next published release must use 0.2.0 because the enum changes break downstream exhaustive matches.
- **Reason:** The HIR already has dedicated closure and closure-parameter structures, while its owner rules keep closure bodies in the containing function's HIR and `HirId` space. Recovering parameter-local parser errors follows the general `Symbol::ERROR` rule and keeps valid parameters and independently recoverable body expressions available. Requiring one definition table for the owner and its nested paths prevents foreign-table IDs. Counting async blocks alongside closures follows the shared preorder rule in §2.8.2 and makes their IDs stable relative to the complete owner syntax, including nested definitions under different parents. Skipping a nested item's syntax keeps its separate owner boundary from consuming the enclosing owner's ordinals or aborting recovery of the enclosing closure body. A wildcard parameter still needs the schema-required `BindingId`, so its binding maps to the written underscore even though it has no HIR `Name`. The repository has no release tags and remains in pre-1.0 development; deferring the version bump until publication avoids treating this implementation commit as a release while making the required semver boundary explicit.
- **Review:** Hostile reviews found and drove fixes for parameter-name and separator recovery, errors under invalid nested items, the ineffective initial mixed-recovery regression, a conflict in the def-path ordinal documentation, and the public error-enum compatibility boundary. The final reviewer confirmed that the mixed parameter/type-annotation test exercises the closure-specific predicate, §2.8.2 defines shared owner-wide preorder ordinals, and the 0.2.0 publication boundary is recorded; no actionable findings remain. All 145 HIR tests and all 619 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `_` closure parameters should retain a `BindingId` and source mapping while exposing `name: None`; that parameter-list recovery should preserve valid parameters and independently recoverable body contents; that syntax beneath a nested item should not shift the current owner's closure ordinals; that lowering should require the owner's existing `DefPathInterner`; and that the next published release should be 0.2.0 due to the non-exhaustive error-enum change.

### IR-147: Lower async-block expressions

- **Decision:** Lower `AsyncBlockExpr` to `ExprKind::AsyncBlock`, preserving the safe source block, `move` versus inferred capture, and the nested `DefId` already assigned in the shared closure/async owner preorder. Generalize nested-definition preparation so an expression containing async blocks but no closures still receives those paths. Preflight the body before allocating HIR or interning paths, and require the owner's `DefPathInterner`. Emit the Stage 5 HIR shape from this low-level API; feature gating, async typing, and coroutine MIR remain separate work.
- **Reason:** The syntax AST and HIR already model async blocks and `.await`, and §2.9.15 specifies their exact HIR shape.
