A shared path-preparation pass keeps closure and async identities stable even when lowering begins at an async-only expression. The repository does not yet have the stage-aware type-checking and coroutine pipeline that enforces the Stage 5 feature diagnostic, so those responsibilities stay outside this syntax-to-HIR function.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover inferred and move capture, body safety and values, async-only owner-preorder paths, a nested async block below a closure, source mappings, missing-interner failure before HIR allocation, and unsupported-body rejection before path interning. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 148 HIR tests and all 622 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that low-level HIR lowering should construct the Stage 5 async-block form regardless of the eventual compiler stage, with stage gating enforced by the higher-level pipeline.

### IR-148: Lower `for await`

- **Decision:** Desugar `for await` directly over its source stream, binding synthetic mutable `%stream` without an `IntoIterator` conversion. Build a `ForAwait` outer match and loop; the loop tail awaits `lang::StreamNext(borrow mut %stream)` and matches `Option.Some(pattern)` against the source block or a synthesized `Option.None` break arm. Mark generated nodes with `SpanCtxt::Desugared(ForAwait)` and preserve source spans for the stream, pattern, block, and label. Give the synthetic `Await` a range covering the `await` keyword so async-context diagnostics point precisely there, while its source-map entry points to the full `ForExpr`. Preflight all source descendants before allocating HIR. As with async blocks, this low-level API emits the Stage 5 HIR form and leaves stage gating and async semantic/MIR behavior to later phases.
- **Reason:** The explicit §2.9.6 shape distinguishes streams from ordinary iterators: stream polling is asynchronous and uses the compiler-internal `StreamNext` marker, while the source stream itself is not converted by `IntoIterator`. The existing HIR has `ForAwait`, `Await`, `StreamNext`, and Option constructor forms, so the documented shape can be represented without new data structures. The stage-aware feature diagnostic and coroutine transform are not implemented in this frontend crate.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that the generated `Await` spanned the whole loop, which would misplace `AWAIT_OUTSIDE_ASYNC`; it now spans the `await` keyword while retaining the full-loop source-map mapping, and the regression test checks both. Follow-up hostile review found no further issues. Focused tests cover direct stream evaluation, hidden mutable binding and source mapping, the `Await(StreamNext(borrow mut %stream))` shape, `ForAwait` sources, Option match arms, source/generated spans, and preflight rejection before allocation. All 149 HIR tests and all 623 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `for await` should consume streams directly without `IntoIterator`, use `StreamNext` under `Await`, point async-context diagnostics at the `await` keyword, and emit this Stage 5 HIR shape before a higher-level stage gate is added.

### IR-149: Lower synchronous function signatures and bodies

- **Decision:** Add `lower_function` for synchronous `FnDecl`s. Lower signature parameters in source order, including `self`, wildcard names, mutability, types, return type, borrow provenances, and effect references. Lower repeated `requires` and `ensures` clauses in written order and store each expression's HIR ID in the corresponding signature list. Inside `ensures`, lower the non-raw single-segment `result` path to `ContractResult` and every call with the non-raw single-segment callee `old` to `ContractOld { args, base }`, preserving malformed arities and argument forms for later diagnostics; outside `ensures` they remain ordinary paths/calls. Lower a source body to a block expression. Give each `FnParam` and its corresponding `BodyParam` a distinct `HirId`, since they are separate signature and body records, and map body bindings to the parameter's name or recovery syntax. Create body parameters only when a body exists or contracts require a body. A bodiless declaration with contracts gets the specified empty-tuple body value, mapped to the declaration span with root context; a bodiless declaration without contracts returns no `Body`. A missing or malformed borrow/effect set lowers to `None`, while an explicitly empty braced set lowers to `Some([])`. Reject async functions before allocation for this slice. Validate that the source-map owner is this declaration, preflight all signature types, contracts, and body expressions before allocation, then prepare nested closure/async-block paths once over the whole owner. The `ContractOld` shape adjustment is a public HIR API change and shares IR-146's next-published-release boundary of 0.2.0.
- **Reason:** `FnSig` and `Body` already define the desired declaration/body split, and keeping contract expressions in written order preserves their obligation order while allowing the signature to reference their expression IDs. Contract built-ins are syntax-contextual, so checking the enclosing `ENSURES_CLAUSE` keeps the same spelling ordinary everywhere else. Preserving all `old(...)` arguments, including named and base arguments, keeps the contract intrinsic identifiable in every syntactically valid shape and gives later semantic analysis enough data to enforce exactly one positional operand without resolving a same-named user function. The signature schema already distinguishes an omitted set from an explicit empty set; malformed syntax must not be normalized to an asserted empty set. Signature parameters describe typed declarations; body parameters introduce the corresponding local bindings, so separate IDs let source maps and later body analyses distinguish the records even though both map to the same parameter syntax. Bodyless foreign or trait declarations do not introduce executable local bindings, while contract-only declarations need a body owner for their predicates. Preparing the full owner before lowering lets nested expressions in both contracts and the body share stable closure/async paths. Async function lowering requires a synthetic async-block parent and dedicated path/span handling, so it remains a separate slice.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that missing `borrows` and `effects` sets collapsed into explicit empty sets, and malformed `old(...)` calls could fall back to ordinary name resolution. Lowering now distinguishes incomplete sets from explicit `{}` and preserves every recognized `old` call as `ContractOld { args, base }`; the follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. Focused regressions cover missing versus empty sets and zero, multiple, named, and base `old` arguments. All 157 HIR tests and all 631 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `FnParam` and `BodyParam` should have distinct HIR IDs while mapping to the same parameter syntax; that a contract-only synthetic tuple should map to the full function declaration with root span context; that bodyless functions without contracts should create no `BodyParam` bindings; that `self` in borrow provenance should be retained as the special interned `self` root; that every non-raw unqualified `old(...)` call should remain a contract-specific HIR node within `ensures`, with its argument shape preserved for later diagnostics; that only valid explicit `{}` sets should be represented as empty; that this public `ContractOld` API adjustment uses the next-published-release boundary 0.2.0; and that async functions should be handled in a separate slice because their generated async block changes lexical definition-parent paths.

### IR-150: Lower async functions through a synthetic async block

- **Decision:** Lower `async fn` signatures with `FnSig.is_async = true`. When an async function has a source body, synthesize the documented outer block and a `move` async block around the source block. Map the generated expression, outer block, and async-block expression to the full function declaration with `SpanCtxt::Desugared(AsyncFn)`; preserve the source body's root span. Give the synthetic async block owner-local ordinal 0, then assign explicit closure/async-block ordinals in source preorder. Nest definitions from the source body below the synthetic async block, while contract definitions remain directly below the function. A bodyless async declaration has no synthetic async block. Preflight all source syntax before interning the synthetic path or allocating HIR. Stage gating, async typing, and MIR transformation remain later work.
- **Reason:** Spec/impl §2.9.15 requires async functions to keep their written output type while returning a future represented by a move-capturing async block. The async block must own all function parameters. Giving this generated owner the first ordinal models its placement at the function head, keeps later IDs deterministic, and makes source-body definitions lexically nested beneath the future while contract closures remain in the function's scope. The low-level HIR API already constructs Stage 5 async-block and await nodes; stage gating belongs to the higher-level compiler pipeline.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover signature async state, move capture, synthetic HIR shape and spans, source-body mappings, contract versus body lexical DefPaths, the ordinal 0 convention, missing-interner rejection before allocation, and bodyless async recovery. The first hostile review found that the contract-path assertion allowed an extra async-block parent; the test now checks the exact direct-child path length and prefix. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace -- --quiet` passed (634 tests), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed, and formatting plus `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the synthetic async block should take owner-local ordinal 0 as if introduced at the async function head; that nested definitions in the source body should use it as lexical parent while contract definitions remain under the function; that generated nodes should map to the full function declaration with `AsyncFn` context; and that bodyless async declarations should not receive a synthetic async block.

### IR-151: Lower explicit generic arguments on call callees

- **Decision:** Preserve explicit type and constant generic arguments on direct function and method call callees by lowering them through the existing generic-argument path into `PathSegment::args` or `ExprKind::Field::args`. Recognize direct callees through parenthesized expressions. Reuse the type-argument lowering and source-map spans already used by type paths, retaining argument order. Continue rejecting generic arguments on standalone expression paths and non-called field expressions as unsupported syntax for this slice.
- **Reason:** The HIR already has generic-argument storage on both path segments and fields, and `lower_generic_args` already lowers types, constants, and associated bindings in source order. Reusing it gives calls the same source-map and recovery behavior without adding HIR shapes. This increment covers the explicitly planned generic call sites while leaving first-class generic item-path lowering for a later phase.
- **Review:** HIR tests cover type and constant arguments on generic function and method callees, source-map spans and type-node mapping, parenthesized-callee recognition, recovery through casts and control-flow conditions, and the retained unsupported boundary for standalone generic paths. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 161 HIR tests and all 635 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that explicit type and constant arguments should be supported on direct function or method call callees, including parenthesized callees; that generic arguments on standalone item paths and non-called fields remain a separate lowering slice; and that malformed argument positions should use the same recovery nodes as type-path generic arguments.

### IR-152: Lower generic paths in value positions

- **Decision:** Lower explicit type and constant generic arguments on path expressions in every expression position, including stored or passed function-item values, range endpoints, index expressions, and binary operands. Reuse `lower_generic_args` and keep arguments on `PathSegment::args`; leave explicit generic arguments on non-called field expressions unsupported for a later slice.
- **Reason:** Generic function items can be values independently of invocation, and the HIR path segment already represents their type and constant arguments. Restricting the feature to call callees left semantically identical paths unsupported depending on their surrounding expression. Field expressions have their own `ExprKind::Field::args` behavior and can remain a distinct implementation boundary.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. Focused HIR tests and all workspace tests passed (161 HIR tests; 635 workspace tests). The regressions cover standalone type and constant arguments with source-map type mapping, range and index positions, binary operands, malformed call-argument recovery, and the remaining function-level generic-field preflight boundary. Workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that explicit generic arguments on expression paths should be lowered in all value positions before name and type resolution, while explicit arguments on non-called field expressions remain a separate follow-up.

### IR-153: Lower generic field expressions in value positions

- **Decision:** Lower explicit type and constant generic arguments on field expressions in every expression position, retaining them in `ExprKind::Field::args`. Reuse the existing generic-argument lowering, source spans, and preflight validation for both method callees and field values.
- **Reason:** The HIR already stores generic arguments on every field node, and the lowerer already knew how to populate that field for method callees. A method item or other generic field expression can also occur as a value, so its lowering should not depend on whether a call immediately follows it.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. Focused HIR tests and all workspace tests passed (161 HIR tests; 635 workspace tests). Coverage exercises type and constant arguments, generic-argument spans and type source mapping, and field values nested in return, assignment, let, array, block, if, match, loop, region, unsafe, closure, async-block, and function-body forms. Workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that explicit generic arguments on field expressions should be retained in all value positions as `ExprKind::Field::args`, with later name and type resolution deciding whether each use is a callable method or another field value.

### IR-154: Lower function generic parameters and predicates

- **Decision:** Add `lower_function_with_generics` to return `(FnSig, Generics, Option<Body>)` while preserving the existing public `lower_function` result `(FnSig, Option<Body>)`. The compatibility API retains its historical behavior and does not lower generic bounds or `where` predicates; the full-result API lowers them. Preflight generic and signature syntax, batch-intern generic parameter paths under the function owner, lower parameter bounds and const types before signature parameters, lower the return type before `where` predicates, then walk `where`, `borrows`, `effects`, `requires`, and `ensures` child nodes in written order before the body. Retain the first borrows/effects clause when duplicates are parser-diagnosed. Batch interning validates every symbol session and required path capacity before mutation. Assign DefPath disambiguators among equal parameter-kind/name siblings. Return empty parameter/predicate slices when their syntax is absent, and lower missing const, where-subject, or recovered `Fn`-family parameter slots to `TyKind::Err`. A written `Fn`-family return arrow with a missing type becomes `Some(TyKind::Err)`; an omitted arrow remains `None` for unit return. A malformed trait path recovers to an empty plain path so its valid prefix cannot become a usable bound. If a recovered `FnSugarBound` has no recognized name, lower it as `FnOnce`, the least-callable function trait.
- **Reason:** The HIR schema stores generic declarations on the owning item, and §2.8.2 requires owner-local HIR IDs to follow syntax preorder. The new full-result API lowers these declarations and places `where` IDs after signature nodes, while the compatibility API keeps its previous signature/body behavior and does not newly require an interner just because generics are present. Walking actual clause children keeps full-result HIR IDs in written order even when the parser diagnoses clause ordering. Keeping the first borrows/effects clause follows the existing singular AST role accessors while retaining parser diagnostics for duplicates. Generic parameter identities belong under the function's DefPath and duplicate source names need stable sibling disambiguators. Batch validation prevents a malformed missing name followed by a foreign-session symbol from leaving earlier parameter paths interned. HIR has no error variant for invalid trait bounds or unrecognized function-sugar names; an empty plain trait path discards the invalid prefix, and `FnOnce` communicates the weakest callability promise. The written-arrow distinction preserves unit return versus a diagnosed missing return type. Missing `FnSugarBound` parameter slots become `TyKind::Err`; parser recovery consumes their separator so later valid parameter types stay attached to the bound and retain their source order.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found missing `Fn`-family return recovery, malformed trait paths lowered as valid prefixes, out-of-order `where` IDs, and partial DefPath writes after a session mismatch. Follow-up review found dropped malformed `Fn` parameter slots, a breaking public return-type change, and missing assertions for generic-bound order and retained borrows. The implementation now preserves the old entry-point behavior, provides the full-result API, lowers clauses in written order, retains malformed Fn slots and later types, and keeps the first duplicate borrows/effects clauses. Regression tests cover these findings, including generic-bound < parameter < return < where order, reordered clauses, duplicate retention, failed-batch atomicity, and parser/HIR recovery of `[Err, I32]`. A further hostile review found a combined parameter/return error mapping bug, the compatibility wrapper's accidental new interner requirement, and a broad source-map range for malformed trait paths. The implementation now selects a missing return error after `->`, keeps legacy generic functions usable without a path interner, and maps an empty malformed-path fallback to the malformed path node. The final hostile review found no actionable issues. All 646 workspace tests pass; Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `lower_function` preserves its historical behavior by omitting generic bounds and `where` predicates, while `lower_function_with_generics` returns the complete generic data and requires the owner's `DefPathInterner`; confirm HIR nodes created for function clauses follow written order even after a clause-order diagnostic; confirm the first duplicate borrows/effects clauses are retained; confirm a missing Fn-family slot becomes `TyKind::Err` while the following comma-separated type stays attached and lowers normally; confirm malformed trait paths become empty paths mapped to their path syntax; confirm a written but missing `Fn`-family return type becomes `Some(TyKind::Err)` mapped to the error after `->`; confirm generic path batches fail without partial interner mutation; confirm duplicate same-kind names receive source-order DefPath disambiguators; confirm an unrecognized function-sugar name recovers as `FnOnce`; and confirm explicit empty effect caps remain distinguishable from omitted or malformed caps.

### IR-155: Assemble top-level function HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `HirLoweringContext` and `lower_function_owner`, which resolve a top-level function by `ErasedAstId` from the supplied `ItemTree`, lower its generics, signature, and optional body, and assemble `ItemKind::Fn`, `HirOwner`, and `HirSourceMap`. Bind each collected `ItemTree` to its `AstIdMap` snapshot with private metadata that is excluded from structural equality, and reject mismatched snapshots during owner lowering. Re-derive the complete recognized raw-attribute list from the current snapshot and compare its values, nested argument spans, outer spans, and syntax pointers with the item-tree entry. Normalize invalid raw and reserved identifiers to `Symbol::ERROR` in both ItemTree and HIR recovery. Require `owner` to resolve to the complete caller-supplied expected `DefPath`, whose terminal component is the item's value-namespace symbol.
- **Reason:** `HirOwner` and its source-map representation were defined, and function lowering already produced their main child records, but there was no operation that tied them to current `ItemTree` metadata. A top-level function entry gives a contained owner slice with enough metadata to validate syntax and produce a complete HIR item without inventing a query system or conflating trait/impl method owner variants. ItemTree does not store enough module-path or disambiguator data to reconstruct the full definition path, so the caller derives the expected path and lowering verifies that the supplied `OwnerId` resolves to it exactly. Storing snapshot identity separately keeps stale AST pointers from resolving through a newer map without invalidating structural item-tree equality across body-only edits.
- **Review:** Targeted owner tests cover complete lowering, non-function, stale metadata and snapshot rejection, invalid raw/reserved-name recovery, nested attribute-span rejection, and final-name mismatch. Hostile review found incomplete raw-attribute validation, an undocumented full-path precondition, inconsistent invalid raw-name recovery, unvalidated nested argument spans, and missing ItemTree snapshot binding; those findings were addressed. Final hostile review found no actionable findings. Workspace tests, Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that this API intentionally handles top-level `Function` entries only, requires the caller to derive and supply the complete expected definition path because ItemTree lacks the containing path and sibling disambiguator, and verifies that `OwnerId` resolves exactly to that path. Confirm that it returns the owner and source map together until a query layer is implemented, retains ItemTree snapshot identity outside structural equality, rejects stale names, visibility, and full raw-attribute data before generic DefPath mutation, and recovers invalid raw or reserved identifiers with `Symbol::ERROR`.

### IR-156: Assemble top-level constant HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_const_owner` to resolve top-level `Const` entries through the same snapshot-bound ItemTree validation as functions, verify current visibility and raw attributes, and construct `ItemKind::Const`, an initializer `Body`, and `HirSourceMap`. Preflight the declared type and initializer together before allocating HIR. Lower missing diagnosed types and initializers to `TyKind::Err` and `ExprKind::Err`, preserving a usable owner.
- **Reason:** The HIR schema specifies constants as `ItemKind::Const { ty }` with the initializer expression stored as `Body::value`. Reusing the function owner checks avoids divergent snapshot, attribute, and DefPath validation while expanding owner construction by one independently testable ItemTree kind. Joint preflight prevents an unsupported initializer from leaving an already-lowered type in the arena after owner lowering fails.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover a public attributed constant, literal initializer, item span/source-map owner mapping, and recovery for missing types and initializers. Hostile review found a partial-allocation risk when an unsupported initializer followed a valid type; joint preflight now checks both fields before HIR allocation. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. All 655 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that every syntactically recovered constant owns a body, including when its initializer is represented by `ExprKind::Err`; confirm that a missing type is represented by `TyKind::Err`; and confirm that the caller derives the complete expected owner path, which lowering checks against the resolved `OwnerId`.

### IR-157: Assemble top-level effect HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_effect_owner` for top-level `Effect` entries using the shared snapshot, visibility, and raw-attribute validation. Require `owner` to resolve to the complete caller-supplied expected `DefPath`, whose terminal component is `DefPathData::Effect(name)`. Create `ItemKind::Effect` with no body and lower complete `covers` references in source order. Omitted `covers` and malformed sets both recover to a zero-length list; malformed sets do not retain a valid-looking prefix. Treat reserved and invalid raw identifier segments as malformed even when the CST contains no error node for them.
- **Reason:** The HIR schema already represents effect declarations as an item with covered effect references, and the declaration has no body. An omitted set is defined as empty. There is no error-reference variant in HIR, so clearing a malformed set preserves the parser or lexer as the sole diagnostic source and avoids granting any capability from only a prefix or a reserved/invalid reference in an invalid declaration.
- **Review:** Tests cover a public attributed effect with built-in and qualified references, source-map mapping of effect references, the distinct effect DefPath component, wrong-parent owner rejection, and empty recovery for omitted, structurally malformed, reserved-name, and invalid-raw-name `covers`. Hostile review found that checking only the terminal DefPath component could attach an owner to the wrong parent; all owner APIs now require and verify the complete expected path. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. All 182 HIR tests and all 657 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a malformed `covers` set should lower all-or-nothing to empty while its parser diagnostic remains authoritative, that an omitted set represents no covered capabilities, and that the caller derives the complete expected effect path because ItemTree does not carry the parent path or sibling disambiguator.

### IR-158: Assemble top-level invariant HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_invariant_owner` for top-level `Invariant` entries with the shared snapshot, visibility, raw-attribute, and complete expected-`DefPath` checks. Build `ItemKind::Invariant` with a plain, one-segment target path; retain the declaration expressions as `Body::invariant_preds` in source order and give the body a synthetic unit value. Preflight every predicate before HIR allocation, then prepare nested closure and async-block paths across the entire declaration so definition ordinals do not restart at each predicate. Keep diagnosed empty or incomplete predicate lists as owners with the predicates the parser retained.
- **Reason:** The HIR schema assigns invariants an owner body whose main value is unit and whose predicate list carries the actual expressions. The grammar names one target type and a source-ordered expression list. Preparing nested definitions over the declaration preserves the same owner-wide preorder used for function bodies, while parser diagnostics remain the authority for incomplete syntax.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover target and predicate source mappings, body shape, empty-list, missing-close, and incomplete-expression recovery, and closure path ordinals across predicates. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 185 HIR tests and all 660 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that invariant lowering should retain diagnosed predicates without adding a body parameter for `self`, represent `self` through the ordinary HIR self-value path root, and defer target-type, placement, uniqueness, and predicate-type checks to semantic validation.

### IR-159: Assemble top-level test HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_test_owner` for top-level `Test` entries with the shared snapshot, visibility, raw-attribute, and complete expected-`DefPath` checks. Require the AST entry to contain its grammar-required string literal. Decode it once; use it for `ItemKind::Test`, the interned item name, and the `DefPathData::Test` component. Lower a present block into the body and a diagnosed missing block to `ExprKind::Err`. Preflight the entire block and prepare nested closure and async-block paths across the whole declaration before lowering.
- **Reason:** The HIR schema models tests as ordinary item owners with a string name and a body. ItemTree stores the decoded test name in the symbol interner, while the stable definition path uses the decoded string directly. Keeping those representations in sync requires validating the current name against the item-tree entry before returning the owner. The grammar recognizes `test` as a declaration only when followed by a string literal; a missing literal remains parser unsupported-item recovery and cannot produce a test owner.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover escaped-name decoding, item and body source mappings, nested closure paths, and missing-body recovery. Hostile review found the initial nameless-test fallback unreachable under the grammar; lowering now rejects a name-less inconsistent entry rather than promising that recovery. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. All 187 HIR tests and all 662 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `test` without a string literal remains outside the `TestDecl` grammar and that semantic test-name and test-body checks remain deferred.

### IR-160: Assemble top-level property HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_property_owner` with the shared snapshot, visibility, raw-attribute, and complete expected-`DefPath` checks. Decode the property string once for its item name and `DefPathData::Property`. Retain each typed declaration parameter as `FnParam` in `ItemKind::Property.params` and create its body-local `BodyParam` separately. Preflight all parameter types and the body before HIR allocation, prepare nested closure and async-block paths across the complete declaration, and lower a diagnosed missing body to `ExprKind::Err`.
- **Reason:** Spec/14 §14.2.2 requires property parameters to carry a concrete type and later checks that type against `Arbitrary`. The previous HIR schema stored only `BodyParam` in `ItemKind::Property`, which discarded the declared type before that semantic check could run. `FnParam` already models name, mutability, type, and source identity; pairing it with `BodyParam` preserves both the declaration type and body binding without adding type data to every function body parameter.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover escaped-name decoding, typed and wildcard parameters, `var` mutability, declaration/body source-map entries and distinct HIR IDs, a parameterless property, nested closure DefPath ordinals, missing-type/body recovery, and rejection of a non-property owner path. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 191 HIR tests and all 666 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that property declaration parameters should use `FnParam` while `Body.params` separately carries their body bindings, and that generic-parameter, `Arbitrary` bound, result-type, and test-harness checks remain deferred to later stages.

### IR-161: Assemble top-level type-alias HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_alias_owner` for `ItemTreeKind::Alias`, verify the complete owner path in the type namespace, and lower generic parameter definitions, bounds, and the alias type into `ItemKind::Alias`. Preflight generic parameter types/bounds and the alias type before HIR allocation. Keep refined aliases and records assigned to their separate item kinds; lower a diagnosed missing alias type to `TyKind::Err` and return no body.
- **Reason:** The HIR already models aliases with their generic parameters and type, while ItemTree distinguishes plain aliases from refined aliases and records. Reusing the existing generic-parameter lowering preserves type/const parameter identity and bounds, and checking `TypeNs` prevents an alias from being attached to a value-namespace definition.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover generic parameter identity and bounds, alias type and source maps, `TypeNs` path construction, missing-type recovery, and rejection of a wrong namespace or non-alias entry. Hostile review found no actionable issues, including in the shared generic-parameter helper refactor. All 194 HIR tests and all 669 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a missing alias right-hand type should preserve the type owner with `TyKind::Err`, and that type-name resolution, alias cycles, and other semantic checks remain deferred.

### IR-162: Assemble top-level record HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_struct_owner` for `ItemTreeKind::Struct`. Verify the complete owner path in the type namespace, lower generic parameters and `where` predicates, and assemble fields in source order with visibility, type, HIR ID, and field `DefId`. Preflight all generic/where/field types before HIR allocation. Add batch field-path interning with per-name source-order disambiguators; preserve duplicate field declarations for later semantic checking. Lower diagnosed missing field types to `TyKind::Err`, and return no body.
- **Reason:** `ItemKind::Struct` already contains `Generics` and `FieldDef` lists, whose field records require stable definition identities nested below the type owner. The interner previously provided batch identity assignment only for generic parameters and nested closure/async-block components, so records could not yet receive correct `DefPathData::Field` paths. Atomic field batches validate symbol sessions and path capacity before mutating the interner.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that a diagnosed `TypeDecl` with neither an `AliasDef` nor a `StructDef` could enter ItemTree's fallback `Alias` category and be fabricated as an error-typed alias. `lower_alias_owner` now requires an actual `AliasDef`; a missing right-hand type within that definition still recovers as `TyKind::Err`. A regression test covers the fallback case, and follow-up hostile review found no additional issues. The full workspace test suite passed all 675 tests, including all 200 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that duplicate field names remain separate source-ordered HIR fields with disambiguated paths, that a missing field type preserves the field with `TyKind::Err`, and that duplicate-field, field-attribute, and field-type semantics remain deferred.

### IR-163: Assemble top-level refined-type HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_refined_owner` for `ItemTreeKind::Refined`. Verify the complete type-namespace owner path, lower the base type into `ItemKind::Refined`, and lower the refinement expression as the owner body's value. Keep the implicit `self` out of `Body.params`; the refined type's base supplies its semantic type. Recover diagnosed missing base types and predicates as `TyKind::Err` and `ExprKind::Err`. Do not lower generic parameter declarations because the refined HIR node has no generic field and the language rejects generic refined types; keep their ItemTree entries available for `GENERIC_REFINED_TYPE` validation.
- **Reason:** This follows the HIR schema and specs 05 §5.6.4 and 09 §9.2.1, which define a refined type as a nominal base-plus-predicate owner whose predicate sees `self` with the base type. ItemTree already retains generic parameter children, so lowering need not invent HIR generic identities for syntax that is invalid by specification.
- **Review:** Hostile review against specs 05 §5.6.4 and 09 §9.2.1 found no actionable issues. Tests cover a generic refined declaration retained for later validation, implicit `self`, base and predicate source maps, missing base/predicate recovery, and wrong owner kinds/paths. All 679 workspace tests passed, including all 204 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that later semantic validation can read generic refined parameter entries from ItemTree to report `GENERIC_REFINED_TYPE`, and that `self` remains an implicit refined-owner binding rather than a `BodyParam`.

### IR-164: Assemble top-level distinct-type HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_distinct_owner` for `ItemTreeKind::Distinct`. Lower generic parameters and the base type into `ItemKind::Distinct`, retain inner constructor visibility in `pub_ctor`, and set `has_predicate` from the presence of a refinement clause. When present, lower its expression as the owner's body value and keep `self` implicit. Recover missing base types and predicates as `TyKind::Err` and `ExprKind::Err`.
- **Reason:** This follows the distinct-type HIR schema and spec/05 §5.6.3. Constructor visibility is separate from the item's outer visibility; a refinement clause changes the owner by adding a predicate body without changing its distinct type representation.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no implementation defect but identified that the initial test combined outer and constructor `pub` markers. Added independent cases for a public type with a private constructor and a private type with a public constructor; follow-up review confirmed the coverage and found no other issues. All 685 workspace tests passed, including all 210 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that inner `pub` exposes only the distinct constructor while outer visibility remains the type's module visibility, and that `has_predicate` remains true when the parser diagnoses a missing predicate expression.

### IR-165: Assemble top-level resource HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_resource_owner` for `ItemTreeKind::Resource`. Verify the expected type-namespace owner path, lower resource fields in source order with the existing field identity and visibility rules, and return no body. Recover a parser-diagnosed missing field type as `TyKind::Err`.
- **Reason:** `ItemKind::Resource` already uses `FieldDef`, and resource declarations reuse `FieldDecl` syntax and ItemTree field entries. Reusing the field lowering path keeps source mapping, field `DefId`s, and visibility consistent with records.
- **Review:** Hostile review against spec/05 §5.6.6 and resource visibility rules found no actionable issues. Tests cover field order, visibility, definition paths, source mappings, missing-type recovery, and wrong kinds/paths. All 688 workspace tests passed, including all 213 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that record-style field visibility and `DefPathData::Field` identities apply unchanged to resource fields; resource-specific ownership and borrow restrictions remain semantic checks.

### IR-166: Assemble top-level enum HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_enum_owner` for `ItemTreeKind::Enum`. Lower generic parameters, `where` predicates, and variants in source order; preserve unit, tuple, and record payloads. Batch-intern variants below the enum and record-variant fields below their variant paths, assigning duplicate names source-order disambiguators. Inherit enum visibility for record-variant fields. Store explicit signed discriminants only when representable as `i128`; leave implicit or unrepresentable values as `None` for later semantic validation.
- **Reason:** This follows the existing `ItemKind::Enum`, `Variant`, and `VariantShape` schema and spec/05 §5.6.2. Variant/field DefPaths preserve identities needed for later name resolution while retaining duplicate declarations for diagnostics. `HirVariant::discriminant` is `Option<i128>`, so this phase preserves all values that fit its model and leaves enum-range validation to semantic checks that can still inspect each mapped source variant.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that a parser-recovered enum with no variant list was lowered as an empty enum, which is valid syntax and denotes an uninhabited type. Added `MissingRequiredSyntax` recovery and a regression test; this preserves the distinction and returns before interning any variant paths. Follow-up hostile review found no further actionable issues. The focused regression test and all 696 workspace tests passed, including 221 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that explicit discriminants outside `i128` should remain `None` with their source available for later diagnostics, and that record-variant fields inherit enum visibility in HIR.

### IR-167: Assemble top-level trait HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_trait_owner` for `ItemTreeKind::Trait`. Lower safety, explicit generic parameters, supertrait bounds, and `where` predicates; retain associated type and function owners in source order. Add an atomic DefPath batch API for `AssocType` and `AssocFn` children, with duplicate disambiguators scoped to each path component. Reject a missing item list so it cannot be mistaken for a valid empty trait.
- **Reason:** This follows the `ItemKind::Trait` schema and spec/05 §5.8. A trait item's associated declarations have separate owners and DefPaths, while the trait owner stores only their `OwnerId`s. The `Generics` HIR field represents source-declared type/const parameters; implicit `Self` has no source declaration and remains implicit for later trait-definition construction.
- **Review:** Hostile review requested negative cases for the wrong ItemTree kind and a mismatched full DefPath, and required checking that no associated paths are partially interned. Added both cases; follow-up hostile review found no further actionable issues. All 701 workspace tests passed, including 226 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that implicit `Self` stays out of the source-backed generic-parameter list and that later trait-definition construction supplies its semantic role; confirm that associated item identities use separate `AssocType` and `AssocFn` path components with duplicate names numbered within each kind; and confirm that a missing item list must not be represented as an empty trait.

### IR-168: Lower associated trait item owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_trait_item_owner` for direct associated type and function declarations. Require the supplied HIR parent to be a trait, resolve both the trait and associated AST IDs from the current ItemTree, verify the parent's source span identifies that exact trait declaration, and verify the child belongs directly to the trait item list. Check that the parent's source-ordered associated `OwnerId` list selects the supplied owner at the matching position, and that its complete DefPath is one component below the trait with the expected `AssocType`/`AssocFn` name and source-order disambiguator. Lower associated type bounds; lower associated function signatures, generics, contracts, and optional default bodies through `lower_function_with_generics`. `has_body` reflects a written body, while a bodyless method with contracts retains its synthetic contract body.
- **Reason:** `TraitItem` is already a distinct owner node in HIR, and the trait owner created in IR-167 stores only the associated owners in source order. Validating the AST-to-owner position prevents duplicate declarations from being attached to one another's DefPaths. Reusing the full function lowerer preserves the same generics, contract, body, and source-map behavior as top-level functions. The HIR's `has_body` field describes default-method status, which must remain false when only synthetic contract storage exists.
- **Review:** Adversarial review found that two same-named traits could let one trait's AST item be lowered under the other's HIR owner. Lowering now requires the parent trait HIR span to match the trait declaration resolved from the current ItemTree; a regression covers cross-assignment between duplicate trait declarations. Follow-up review found no remaining actionable issues. Tests cover associated type bounds and source mapping, generic required methods, default method bodies, contract-only bodiless methods, duplicate owner/path ordering, wrong expected paths, and mismatched parents. All 703 workspace tests passed, including 228 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that associated declaration identity follows source order even for duplicate names, that a bodiless trait method may own a synthetic body for its contracts while `has_body` stays false, and that trait/impl contract compatibility and associated-type semantics remain later validation work.

### IR-169: Assemble top-level impl HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_impl_owner` for ItemTree impl entries. Require and validate the full expected `DefPath` ending in the entry's header hash, including the source-order disambiguator among identical header hashes; lower source generic parameters, `where` predicates, safety, and the inherent, trait, or interface target. Select trait-impl recovery from the written `for` token even when the parser produced neither a trait reference nor a self type. Preserve trait-reference generic arguments and associated-type bindings. Batch-intern impl associated type/function paths in source order. Accept an empty `ITEM_LIST` but reject a missing list. Recover a missing self type as `TyKind::Err`; because `ImplKind::Trait` can only store a `Path`, lower a malformed or non-path trait reference as an empty path tied to its syntax, and a missing trait reference as an empty path tied to the impl syntax. Lower `ModPath` interface targets as plain, nongeneric paths.
- **Reason:** `ItemKind::Impl` and `ImplKind` already represent all three grammar forms, and ItemTree retains both the header hash and mixed generic/associated declaration children. The header hash gives impl identity independently of its body, while separate associated owner IDs keep method bodies and associated-type definitions in their own HIR owners.
- **Review:** Adversarial review found that identical headers could be cross-wired under the first impl's DefPath. The lowerer now derives the canonical disambiguator from the ItemTree source order and rejects the mismatch before interning associated paths; a regression verifies both the rejection and successful lowering under each correct path. Follow-up review found that `impl for {}` fell through to inherent-impl recovery because both type accessors were absent. The lowerer now uses the direct `for` token to select trait-impl recovery, covered by a regression for missing trait and self types. Final adversarial review found no further actionable issues. All 706 workspace tests passed, including 231 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm empty-path recovery for malformed trait references given the current HIR schema, that the impl `DefPath` terminal component uses the stable ItemTree header hash with a source-order disambiguator for identical headers, and that coherence, visibility restrictions, required trait items, and interface conformance remain semantic checks.

### IR-170: Lower associated impl item owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_impl_item_owner` for direct `AssocTypeDef` and `FnDecl` children. Verify that the parent HIR owner and full DefPath identify the current impl, that the child is directly in its item list, that the source-ordered parent owner list selects the requested child owner, and that the child DefPath is one canonical component below the impl. Lower associated type right-hand sides, recovering a missing type as `TyKind::Err`; lower impl functions through `lower_function_with_generics`, preserving source visibility and whether a body was written.
- **Reason:** `ImplItem` is a distinct HIR owner kind, while `ItemKind::Impl` stores only the associated `OwnerId`s. Resolving parent and child identity together prevents same-named impls or duplicate member names from cross-wiring method bodies or associated type definitions.
- **Review:** Adversarial review found no actionable issues after checking parent/child identity, source-order owner mapping, DefPath validation, recovery, and function lowering against the analogous trait-item owner. Focused tests cover valid associated types and generic methods with `where`/`requires`/`ensures`, duplicate member names, missing RHS/body recovery, and a same-header sibling-parent cross-wire. All 709 workspace tests passed, including 234 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a missing associated type right-hand side should remain an error type, that a bodyless impl method retains `has_body = false` while contract-only body storage may remain synthetic and parser diagnostics report the required body, and that visibility legality, duplicate member checking, coherence, and impl conformance remain later validation work.

### IR-171: Assemble interface HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_interface_owner` for top-level interface declarations. Validate the current ItemTree entry and complete interface `DefPath`, including the source-order disambiguator among same-named interfaces; require an `ITEM_LIST`, and intern direct member owners atomically in source order. Map type declarations, enums, and resources to `TypeNs` components, and interface functions to `ValueNs`, disambiguating equal components within their namespace.
- **Reason:** `ItemKind::Interface` stores only its child owner IDs, and `InterfaceItem` is a separate owner kind. A mixed batch is necessary because an interface can contain type and value names with distinct namespaces; doing single-path writes would risk leaving partial state on failure.
- **Review:** Adversarial review found that same-named interfaces could be cross-wired under the first interface's DefPath. The lowerer now derives the canonical disambiguator from ItemTree source order and rejects the mismatch before interning child paths; a regression verifies rejection and successful lowering under both correct paths. Follow-up adversarial review found no further issues. All 713 workspace tests passed, including 238 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm `TypeNs` versus `ValueNs` for direct interface members, source-order disambiguation for same-named interfaces and duplicate members, and that member contents, interface restrictions, and resource-member owners remain separate lowering/validation work.

### IR-172: Lower direct interface item owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_interface_item_owner` for direct records, aliases, refined types, enums, resources, and interface functions. Validate the parent HIR interface against the current ItemTree/AST declaration, including source span, visibility, attributes, complete DefPath, and parent owner slot; validate direct list membership and the child's namespace component and canonical source-order disambiguator. Lower all supported item data into `InterfaceItemKind`. Force record and record-variant fields to `Pub`. Keep refined clauses as spans and bodies absent. Lower interface function signatures and generics without retaining forbidden contracts or bodies. When lowering a resource owner, intern its direct `AssocFn` paths in source order so separate resource-member owners can be lowered next.
- **Reason:** `InterfaceItem` is a separate HIR owner family with a fixed schema, and `ItemKind::Interface` stores only child owner IDs. The checks bind the supplied child to one source declaration and parent slot, which prevents duplicate names or sibling interfaces from borrowing each other's owner identity. Interface records are public by spec; interface/world declarations have no bodies. The signature-only entry point preserves shared signature/generic lowering while honoring that body and contract syntax is diagnosed and discarded from interface HIR.
- **Review:** Adversarial review found no actionable issues after checking parent/child cross-wiring, snapshot and metadata validation, namespace/disambiguator checks, resource-member path creation, and signature-only body/contract handling. Regressions cover every direct item kind, public record/variant fields, duplicate direct and resource member names, invalid generic/body/contract recovery, and a same-name sibling-interface parent mismatch. All 716 workspace tests passed, including 241 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that record and record-variant fields are public even without a `pub` marker; that invalid interface generic declarations remain in HIR for later `GENERIC_INTERFACE_ITEM` checks; that refined interface declarations retain only their base and clause span because interface validation rejects the clause; and that resource-member DefPaths are created with the resource owner even though member owners are lowered separately.

### IR-173: Lower interface resource-member owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_interface_resource_member_owner` for a resource's direct `AssocFn` children. Validate the interface and resource declarations against the current ItemTree/AST snapshot, verify both parent owner slots and direct syntax-list membership, and require the complete path below the resource with the canonical duplicate-name disambiguator. Classify a member as `Method` when its first lowered signature parameter has `is_self`; otherwise classify it as `Constructor` only when it is named `new` and returns exactly `Self` or its resource type; classify every other member as `Static`. Pass the three source AST identities in `InterfaceResourceMemberIds`. Lower signature and generic data while leaving the owner body absent and omitting forbidden contract IDs.
- **Reason:** Resource-member paths are created when the resource owner is assembled, while each function still requires an independently addressable `InterfaceItem` owner. Checking interface→resource→member identity prevents an owner from borrowing a sibling resource's source or path. Using lowered `FnParam::is_self` follows the role rule directly. Hostile review caught a mismatch between the initial implementation-plan summary and normative spec/13 §13.2.4: a `new` returning `Result<Self, E>` is explicitly Static. The implementation plan's §§16.5.1 and 16.6.2 now state that normative rule. Retaining generic signature data lets later interface validation diagnose prohibited generics without losing the declaration. Grouping the three AST IDs also keeps the owner API below Clippy's argument limit and prevents positional mixups.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found the `new`-return classification mismatch and requested stronger checks for the retained generic signature; both are addressed. Focused regressions cover methods, constructors returning both `Self` and the resource name, `new -> Result<Self, E>` as Static, duplicate `AssocFn` paths, retained parameter/return/generic/where data, omitted `requires`/`ensures` and bodies, and rejection of a member cross-wired to a sibling resource. The final hostile review found no actionable issues. All 718 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the member-role definition in normative spec/13 §13.2.4 takes precedence over the earlier shorthand in impl/16 §16.5.1 and the constructor validation wording in §16.6.2: a `new` with a missing or non-`Self`/resource return is Static. Also confirm that the first lowered parameter's `is_self` flag is the method discriminator and that diagnosed resource-member generics remain in HIR while forbidden contracts and bodies do not.

### IR-174: Assemble world HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_world_owner` for top-level `World` entries. Validate current snapshot metadata, the complete world `DefPath`, and its canonical same-name source-order disambiguator derived from current top-level AST declarations. Retain direct import, export, and include owner IDs in source order and atomically intern each `WorldItem { import, name }` or `WorldInclude(name)` child path with a per-component disambiguator. Re-derive every child name from its current function name or `WorldRef` and compare it with ItemTree before path interning. Accept an explicit empty list and reject a missing list.
- **Reason:** `ItemKind::World` stores only child owner IDs, while `WorldItem` and `WorldInclude` have distinct HIR owner kinds and DefPath components. Import/export direction is part of `WorldItem` identity; repeated paths or function names in one direction still require disambiguation. ItemTree's snapshot binding validates AST pointer identity but does not make public entry values immutable, so both child names and same-name world order must come from current syntax. Preparing the complete mixed list before calling `intern_children` prevents partial path state after metadata, name, or path errors.
- **Review:** Hostile review found two gaps: child names were copied from ItemTree without comparison to current syntax, and same-name world disambiguation trusted mutable ItemTree names/order. Lowering now re-derives and checks package/module references, function names, include names, and world sibling identity/order from the current AST before interning. Focused tests cover package and module references, import/export functions and references, includes, colliding imports with the same visible name, independent include disambiguators, empty versus missing lists, duplicate world names, reordered/tampered ItemTree siblings, and stale child-name rejection before path mutation. The final adversarial review found no further actionable issues. All 721 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that import direction participates in world-item identity, include paths use their separate `WorldInclude` component, equal components are numbered in source order, and an explicit empty list differs from a missing list.

### IR-175: Lower world-item owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_world_item_owner` for direct import, export, and include entries. Validate the current world parent, its exact AST/ItemTree identity, source span, metadata, full DefPath and source-ordered owner slot; validate the child node, entry kind, name, and complete one-component DefPath with its source-order disambiguator. Lower package references to `WorldRef::Pkg` with the HIR ID mapped to the enclosing `WorldRef`; lower module references to `WorldRef::Mod` through `lower_mod_path`. Lower import/export function parameters and return types into `FnSig`, with no body or contract IDs. Omit generic parameters and `where` predicates because world-function HIR stores only `FnSig`, and the grammar already diagnoses them. For a present but malformed `WorldRef` with neither a package token nor a usable module path, retain the owner using an empty module path mapped to that reference node.
- **Reason:** `WorldItem` is a separate owner associated with a world, while the world itself retains the child `OwnerId`s in source order. Parent and child validation prevents cross-wiring duplicate items or same-named worlds. The shared function lowerer normally requires the owner root to be the `FnDecl`; world imports/exports instead own an outer `WorldImport`/`WorldExport` node, so a private nested-signature route allows the signature's source mappings to stay in the WorldItem owner. The HIR schema has no place to retain invalid generic declarations, and parser diagnostics already identify them.
- **Review:** An adversarial review initially suggested mapping `WorldRef::Mod`'s `Path.hir_id` to the `MOD_PATH` child. Rechecking impl/16 §16.8.1 confirms that this ID is the `WorldRef` resolution key, so the implementation intentionally maps it to the enclosing `WORLD_REF`; the reviewer withdrew the finding. Tests now assert that mapping, map each path segment to its `NAME_REF`, check the exact package-reference span, and cover malformed function parameter/return recovery. Other focused coverage verifies both function directions, includes, body/contract omission, duplicate-child and sibling-world cross-wiring, stale ItemTree names, canonical disambiguators, parser-recovered malformed references, and diagnosed generic/where syntax. The final adversarial review found no actionable issues. All 725 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that diagnosed world-function generics and `where` clauses need not remain in HIR, and that an empty module path is an acceptable recovery representation for a malformed reference given the current `WorldRef` schema.

### IR-176: Assemble extern-block HIR owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_extern_block_owner` for top-level extern blocks. Validate current ItemTree metadata, the full `ExternBlock { abi, module }` DefPath, and the canonical disambiguator among equal block headers derived from current AST source order. Retain direct extern-function owners in source order and atomically intern their `ValueNs` paths with per-name source-order disambiguators. Lower the safety marker, decoded ABI and optional module; map `"wasm"` and `"C"` to their HIR ABI variants and other decoded ABI strings to `ExternAbi::Err`. Accept explicit empty item lists and keep the block bodiless.
- **Reason:** `ItemKind::ExternBlock` stores the ABI, module, safety, and child owner IDs, while every extern function is a separate owner. The ABI and module are also part of the block's stable DefPath identity. Comparing current syntax with ItemTree before batching paths prevents stale child names or reordered duplicate headers from cross-wiring owner IDs; the batch avoids partially interning children when validation fails.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that parser-retained `pub`/`export` markers made the AST visibility disagree with ItemTree's fixed private visibility and blocked recovery. The lowerer now uses the schema's private visibility while retaining parser diagnostics; regression tests lower both diagnosed forms under canonical duplicate-block paths. Other tests cover escaped ABI/module decoding, Wasm/C/unknown ABI recovery, duplicate function names, duplicate block ordering after ItemTree reordering, and stale child-name rejection before path interning. Follow-up adversarial review found no further actionable issues. All 729 workspace tests passed, including 254 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that an unknown but decoded ABI should remain in the DefPath while the block's HIR ABI is `Err`, and that Stage 7 gating and extern-function signature validity remain later diagnostics rather than owner-assembly failures.

### IR-177: Lower extern-item owners

- **Decision:** Add `ExternItemIds` and `lower_extern_item_owner` for a direct extern function. Validate the parent block's current AST and ItemTree metadata, raw attributes, private visibility, HIR ABI/module/safety fields, source span, complete DefPath, and same-header source-order disambiguator. Verify direct child membership, the parent's source-ordered owner slot, and the child's complete one-component `ValueNs` path with its duplicate-name disambiguator. Lower function generics, signature, `where` predicates, and contracts through `lower_function_with_generics`; parser-diagnosed bodies remain absent.
- **Reason:** Extern functions have independent `ExternItem` owners while their block stores only child `OwnerId`s. Parent and slot checks prevent same-named functions in sibling blocks from borrowing one another's owner identity. Full function lowering retains invalid-but-diagnosed signature forms for semantic diagnostics, and the HIR body slot stores contract expressions when present even though an import has no executable body.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. Focused regressions cover generic and `where` data, both contract forms and their synthetic body, duplicate function-name paths, missing signature types, diagnosed function bodies, sibling-block cross-wiring, wrong owner slots, and stale ItemTree child names before any path mutation. All 732 workspace tests passed, including 257 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that contracts on imports use the signature's `requires`/`ensures` IDs plus the `HirOwner.body` contract-only representation; confirm that block safety/ABI restrictions and diagnosed extern-function forms remain later semantic validation.

### IR-178: Validate `@derive` attributes

- **Decision:** Preserve whether each raw word argument was lexed as `UIDENT` or `LIDENT`; validate all 19 fixed derive names into `ValidatedAttrs.derives` in source order; report and drop duplicate arguments; drop the full derive attribute for an unknown name and offer a unique Damerau–Levenshtein replacement over Unicode scalar values; provide a machine-applicable merge fix for a repeated `@derive`. The merge carries comments from every trivia gap in the removed attribute and follows the file's first-line ending convention. Keep `IMPLEMENTED_ATTRIBUTE_STAGE` at 0. Correct §2.9.17 of the implementation plan to exclude resources, which spec/15 requires to receive `DERIVE_NOT_SUPPORTED`.
- **Reason:** The lexer distinguishes simple words from plain type-style names, so retaining token class is necessary to reject lowercase and raw forms as the specified `UIDENT` argument grammar requires. Implementing Stage 2 validation behind a testable stage parameter makes the feature ready without enabling Stage 1 and Stage 2 production attributes that remain incomplete. The normative attribute and type specs take precedence over the implementation-plan sentence that incorrectly listed resources as derive owners.
- **Review:** Adversarial review identified four initial gaps: an unknown derive was partially retained, typo suggestions were missing, duplicate derive attributes had no merge fix, and raw arguments lost the `UIDENT`/`LIDENT` distinction. After those were fixed, follow-up review found mixed-line-ending drift, dropped comments between the attribute name and `(`, and byte-based typo distance; a later check found comments between `@` and the name also needed preservation. Each finding was fixed and regression-tested. The latest requested pass after the final trivia fix had not returned at the time of this record. The workspace test suite, workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the Stage 0 production gate remains until all earlier-stage attributes are implemented, and that the normative specs govern resource derives and Unicode edit distance.

### IR-179: Lower `DerivedImpl` owners

- **Decision:** Add `lower_derived_impl_owners` for record, enum, distinct, and refined declarations at top level or inside an interface. Re-run Stage 2 validation against the current ItemTree attributes, target kind, and interface context, and require the complete derive list to equal the supplied `ValidatedAttrs`. Verify the source declaration, parent HIR owner, full type DefPath, and canonical same-name source-order disambiguator before interning child paths. Create one bodyless `DerivedImpl` owner per derive, with its trait-name child DefPath, `self_def`, desugared trait-name span, and source map rooted at that `NameRef`. Keep resource declarations excluded as required by spec/15.
- **Reason:** A derived implementation is a distinct HIR owner below its type, while its generated header and method shims belong to later semantic and MIR phases. Revalidating the complete attribute result prevents stale or incomplete validation data from generating an impl that the current source does not support. Source-derived disambiguators keep duplicate type declarations and interface members tied to their own owner paths.
- **Review:** Initial hostile review found that an unknown name added to a current derive list could be missed if a prior `ValidatedAttrs` value were reused, and that duplicate-name path disambiguators lacked coverage. Lowering now revalidates the complete current derive list and rejects stale results before interning; regressions cover changed-to-invalid attributes, duplicate top-level type names, and same-named record/enum interface members. Final adversarial review found no further actionable issues. All workspace tests passed, including 266 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the generated impl header, kind-specific `DERIVE_NOT_SUPPORTED` rules, field and supertrait obligations, and generated method shims remain owned by impl/03 and impl/04; confirm that a derived owner itself has no body or attributes and points `self_def` at the type declaration.

### IR-180: Filter ItemTrees by `@target`

- **Decision:** Add recursive `TargetPredicate::matches` evaluation over a target name and the driver's effective capability set. `TargetCfg::new` validates the target/capability combination, including requiring `memory64` for wasm64 targets and rejecting it elsewhere. Add `ItemTree::filter_for_target` to validate target-only attributes on module-level declarations and `use` entries, preserve invalid or non-matching-context targets with diagnostics, and remove valid false matches with their nested children. Before using attributes, verify snapshot/root/source identity and compare against a fresh collection; return target validation results for retained entries. Add `ItemTree::collect_for_target` as the combined entry point a compiler collector must use before name binding and duplicate checks.
- **Reason:** `@target` changes which declarations exist and must run before binding or duplicate checks. The build driver owns artifact-dependent capabilities such as `host`, so the HIR layer consumes that resolved set instead of reconstructing artifact policy. Fresh source collection prevents mutated or stale ItemTree metadata from controlling the build graph.
- **Review:** Initial adversarial review found that no current production call site invoked filtering and that a test supplied `memory64` for `wasm32-core`. Added the combined `collect_for_target` entry point; the workspace contains only frontend, HIR, diagnostics, interning, stable-hash, syntax, and VFS crates, with no package collector, name resolver, query engine, or compiler driver to wire. Replaced public `TargetCfg` fields with a validating constructor, corrected the test to use valid wasm64 configuration, and added rejection tests for unknown and target-incompatible capabilities. Follow-up adversarial review passed with no actionable findings. All workspace tests passed, including 273 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** When a package collector or compiler driver is added, route its build collection through `collect_for_target` before every binding and duplicate check. Confirm that it supplies artifact-correct capabilities (especially `host`, `native_ffi`, and `process`) and merges target-only validation with remaining attribute validation exactly once.

### IR-181: Validate attributes on retained module entries

- **Decision:** Run target-only attribute validation first. For each retained top-level item or `use`, validate its full recognized attribute list and return the resulting `ValidatedAttrs` keyed by `ErasedAstId`. Derive module-level kind for every item, `has_body` from the current top-level function AST, and `is_exported` from ItemTree visibility. Suppress every attribute diagnostic from false-target entries.
- **Reason:** Validating unrelated attributes before `@target` would diagnose source that does not exist for the selected build. Returning the full result for retained items gives a future attributes query a current, source-bound value to merge without repeating decoding. The public attribute stage remains 0; later-stage attributes continue to report `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`.
- **Review:** Adversarial review found no correctness issue and requested an export-context regression test because current stage-0 attributes do not distinguish exported functions. Added that regression. Focused and full workspace tests pass (274 `sai_hir` tests); workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the future `attrs(DefId)` query consumes the returned result once, and extend `AttributeTarget` derivation for trait/impl/interface/extern members before validating their contextual attributes. Confirm that export status and body presence continue to come from current source.

### IR-182: Validate attributes across retained ItemTrees

- **Decision:** Recursively validate recognized attributes for every retained ItemTree entry with a valid target, including nested members, record/resource fields, and variants, while preserving target-first filtering at module scope. Derive function kind/body, export status, enclosing impl kind, extern ABI/safety, and interface/trait membership from the current AST. For generic parameters, record-variant fields, world entries, and world includes, retain an empty validated result and let the parser own the single `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET` diagnostic required by §15.2.1. Keep this distinction private to ItemTree traversal rather than adding a sentinel to the public `TargetKind`.
- **Reason:** Stage 0 validation applies to declarations throughout the item tree, and contextual rules such as `@inline`, `@deprecated`, and interface `@repr` depend on the actual enclosing declaration. Revalidating grammar-prohibited positions in HIR duplicates parser errors and can emit unrelated stage diagnostics for attributes that the parser already treats as absent. A false-target parent must suppress attribute diagnostics from all children because none of them participate in that build.
- **Review:** Focused ItemTree target tests cover retained nested attributes, trait and inherent/trait-impl distinctions, interface `@repr`, extern context, parser-owned diagnostics at prohibited positions, and suppression beneath a filtered parent. The first adversarial review found that variant fields were incorrectly treated as valid `field` targets, parser-owned invalid-position diagnostics could be duplicated (including a later-stage feature error), and a proposed sentinel would expand the public `TargetKind` API. All three findings were fixed by skipping parser-rejected positions with empty results and keeping the distinction private. Follow-up adversarial review found no remaining actionable issues. All workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the parser remains the sole owner of target diagnostics for generic parameters, record-variant fields, world imports/exports/includes, and function parameters. Connect the retained source-bound values to the eventual `attrs(DefId)` and lint-level queries. Function-parameter attributes are prohibited by spec/15 §15.2.1 and do not need collection.

### IR-183: Diagnose conflicting lint levels on one target

- **Decision:** Track lint-code assignments across all `@allow`, `@warn`, and `@deny` attributes validated for one target. Keep the first level, report `CONFLICTING_LINT_LEVEL` at each later assignment at a different level with a label at the first, and drop the conflicting assignment. Continue to report same-level repeats as `DUPLICATE_ATTRIBUTE_ARGUMENT`.
- **Reason:** Spec/15 §15.15.3 item 4 and N-A-46 require the same conflict rule used by the package `lints` block. The check depends only on one target's attributes and must be applied before the deferred diagnostic-registry lookup.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover the later conflict span, label to the first assignment, retained first level, and a same-level duplicate following a conflicting assignment. Full workspace tests, Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` pass. Adversarial review found no ordering, duplicate, retention, or diagnostic-location issue. It noted that adding `Code::ConflictingLintLevel` extends the public exhaustive `Code` enum and can break downstream exhaustive matches; this is accepted because the normative diagnostic needs its own code and this repository is still at version 0.1.0.
- **Human review:** Confirm that conflicting assignments to unknown spellings still receive the target-local conflict diagnostic before registry classification. IR-184 adds lint-name validation and marks `Code` non-exhaustive so future diagnostic additions do not keep breaking downstream exhaustive matches; review that API transition before the first stable release.

### IR-184: Classify and validate lint names

- **Decision:** Add the complete, sorted v0.1 diagnostic-code name snapshot from Appendix A and expose `lookup_lint_name` independently of the partial `Code::ALL` enum. Add all 28 lint entries with their default levels and stages; classify every other registered code as hard with its fixed severity. In HIR, validate lint names after per-target duplicate and conflict handling, retain only registered lints (including later-stage lints), report `NOT_A_LINT` or `UNKNOWN_LINT` for the others, provide lint-only suggestions, and attach a machine-applicable uppercase fix only when it names a registered lint. Mark `Code` non-exhaustive.
- **Reason:** Attribute names must be checked against the complete registry even when the compiler does not implement the code's stage.
