- **Review:** The new root retains the same tree and author metadata, and its message no longer contains the trailer. The rewritten branch histories contain no matching trailer; old local tips remain reachable from `refs/original`, and remote refs were not updated.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the repository root was the intended meaning of “the first commit”; the first implementation commit on `codex` had no trailer. Confirm that rewriting both local branch histories, and therefore changing descendant commit IDs, was expected.

### IR-223: Validate lock-file semantics

- **Decision:** Add a lock-only semantic validation stage for supported format versions, identities, required and single-occurrence fields, Git/path field compatibility, checksums, normalized paths, canonical Git URLs, known and component targets, requirement uniqueness, root `only` membership, and artifact/import target correspondence. Keep checks that need package contracts, fetched contents, or the resolved graph for the resolver stages. Treat repeated members in set-valued target, `only`, and capability lists as valid noncanonical values for canonical serialization to deduplicate; keep repeated `artifact` and `imports` fields for one target corrupt.
- **Reason:** `docs/package/03-lock-file.md` defines `LOCK_CORRUPT` for malformed lock semantics and separately defines valid noncanonical locks. The validator must not mistake local lock-shape checks for package-contract or graph consistency checks, which the resolver owns. Set-valued lists do not change meaning when a name is repeated, while each artifact/import target field carries one target-specific record.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found missing component-target checks, an omitted WIT checksum for artifact entries, acceptance of empty `only` clauses, and an over-restricted SSH username. Regressions and validation now cover those cases. A follow-up found empty root targets and malformed future-format headers being misclassified; both are fixed, including UTF-8 and bidi checks in header comments. The review also suggested percent-encoded SSH usernames; this was not adopted because N-G-47 rejects percent-encoding in Git URLs, and §4.2.1 now states that the prohibition applies to every URL component. An independent adversarial read confirmed that repeated members of set-valued lists are noncanonical rather than corrupt, while repeated artifact/import target fields are corrupt. A final hostile pass identified three more edge cases: unsupported whitespace in a header comment could be bypassed by the raw version detector, leading blank/comment lines could precede the header despite the first-line rule, and an overflowing format integer needed an explicit boundary. The detector now shares the lexer's whitespace rule; the parser enforces the first-line rule; the contract's unsigned 64-bit `INT` bound is documented and regression-tested. All 120 package tests and 951 workspace tests pass; warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that repeated set members in a lock should be accepted as noncanonical and removed by canonical serialization, while duplicate artifact/import records for a target remain corrupt. Confirm the strict first-line header rule and the unsigned 64-bit ceiling for format integers. Contract-dependent field presence, graph consistency, and fetched-content verification remain for the resolver and distribution stages.

### IR-224: Serialize validated locks canonically

- **Decision:** Add a pure serializer that accepts syntactically and lock-locally valid parsed locks. Emit the format header, root block, sorted package entries, and fields in the documented order; sort by UTF-8 bytes where required; normalize set-valued lists by sorting and removing repeated values; remove capabilities covered by another capability from source footprints; and omit `dir "."` because the canonical form represents the repository root without a `dir` field. Preserve imported interfaces in artifact `imports` sets because those values describe the artifact's direct imports. Return validation diagnostics through a serialization error rather than producing partial output.
- **Reason:** `docs/package/03-lock-file.md` defines canonical bytes as the equality basis for locks and specifies entry, field, and list order. `docs/impl/08-effect-and-capability-analysis.md` §8.7.10 requires capability footprints to remove covered capabilities; artifact imports record direct imports and are not that normalized source footprint. WIT capability existence depends on the resolved graph ([package/01 §1.11.2](../package/01-package-contract.md)), so the resolver must validate names from the original lock before normalization, and must treat `dir "."` as the repository root consistently for tag lookup and checksums.
- **Review:** Tests compare exact canonical output, verify sorting and normalization, preserve direct artifact imports, omit `dir "."`, check fixed-point serialization, and reject syntax or lock-local semantic errors. Hostile review found that empty sets had two spaces (`{  }`) instead of the required `{ }`; the writer now handles empty lists explicitly, with regressions for empty `capabilities` and `imports`. A second pass found malformed future-format locks could return syntax errors before the required unsupported-version result; serialization now preserves the validator's format-version priority, with a regression for `lock 2` missing its root. Independent reviews found that repository-root `dir "."` should canonicalize away and that known WIT capabilities require resolver context; the serializer omits the redundant directory and documents that the resolver must validate original names before normalization. A final review confirmed that `dir "."` must select the same tag and tree as an omitted field; package and resolver specifications now say so. All 124 package tests and 955 workspace tests pass; warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm the accepted noncanonical `dir "."` spelling, omission during serialization, and root semantics for tag lookup and tree hashing. The resolver must reject WIT capabilities unknown to the selected target and resolved package graph before normalization and persistence.

### IR-225: Detect noncanonical lock bytes

- **Decision:** Add `lock_serialization::is_canonical(source)`, which parses the given bytes, requires lock-local syntax and semantic validity, serializes the parsed file canonically, and compares the bytes exactly. Return an error for invalid locks and a boolean for canonical versus valid noncanonical locks. Require resolver callers to validate graph-dependent capability names from the original parsed lock before calling, because normalization may remove covered names.
- **Reason:** `docs/package/03-lock-file.md` defines valid noncanonical locks as a warning and uses canonical serialized bytes for semantic equality. Keeping the raw source bound to parsing inside the helper avoids comparing bytes from one file with a syntax tree parsed from another. Graph-dependent `UNKNOWN_CAPABILITY` checks remain in resolver stage 3.
- **Review:** Tests cover exact canonical bytes, missing canonical layout, duplicate set members, comments, and invalid syntax. This helper reports canonicality only; mapping `false` to the user-facing `LOCK_NOT_CANONICAL` warning remains for resolver integration. All 126 package tests and 957 workspace tests pass; warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a `Result<bool, LockSerializationError>` is suitable here and that the resolver performs all graph checks before this canonicality comparison.

### IR-226: Validate the graph recorded in a lock

- **Decision:** Add a standalone lock-graph validator that requires syntax and lock-local semantic validity, then checks that each requirement points to an entry on the requirement minimum's compatibility line, that the recorded selection equals and satisfies that entry, every entry is reachable from the root, and there are no package cycles or edges back to the root's own node. Use iterative traversal so graph depth does not consume the call stack; return source spans and deterministic cycle paths with graph errors.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.10.2 conditions 4 and 7 define these checks independently of root-contract comparison, source choice, or fetched contents. Those other conditions need resolver inputs not represented by `LockParseOutput`. Keeping graph consistency separate from lock corruption allows a future resolver to choose Mode B when this check fails instead of misreporting `LOCK_CORRUPT`.
- **Review:** Tests cover a valid chain, missing entry, selection mismatch, unsatisfied requirement, unreachable entry, dependency cycle, indirect cycle to the root, distinct compatibility lines for a dependency with the root's identity, and invalid input preconditions. Two independent hostile reviews found that DFS traversal could rotate a reported cycle away from its least identity when entries were listed in another order; canonical rotation was added. A follow-up found that rotating the repeated closing node with the rest could drop a middle node, so rotation now applies only to the unique cycle nodes; two- and three-node regressions cover both cases. Both reviewers found no remaining issue after the fixes. Ten focused tests and all 967 workspace tests pass; warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. This is an API stage only; there is not yet a resolver caller that selects Mode A/Mode B or reports the user-facing diagnostics.
- **Human review:** Confirm that an edge to the root's same identity and compatibility line is reported as a cycle while another line remains a separate node, and that this standalone checker returns Mode-A-ineligibility data without assigning `LOCK_CORRUPT`. Resolver integration must additionally compare the current root contract, pins, and selected sources before accepting Mode A.

### IR-227: Compare the root contract with the lock root block

- **Decision:** Add a comparator for the root identity, version, target set, and included root edges. Compare requirements by their canonical comparator spelling, preserve the `requires test` flag and the distinction between omitted and explicit `only`, and treat target and `only` members as sets. Require the package parse result to have been produced in root role; retain that role in `PackageParseOutput` so the comparator can enforce it. Do not compare source clauses or capability grants here because the lock root block does not store them.
- **Reason:** These are Mode A conditions 2 and 3 in resolver §2.10.2. Package parsing and lock-local validation provide all required data, while sources, grants, path contents, pins, and dependency contracts require later resolver checks. Rejecting a parse performed in dependency role prevents its suppressed root-only duplicate diagnostics from being mistaken for a complete root parse.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover a matching contract, set normalization, root metadata mismatches, test/`only` mismatches, invalid input/role preconditions, and a matching root block whose recorded graph is still invalid. Two independent hostile reviews found the method name could be mistaken for a complete Mode A approval; API documentation now limits it to conditions 2 and 3, and a regression demonstrates that graph validation remains necessary. They also found callers could forge the public parse-role field or transplant a dependency-parsed AST after root-only duplicate diagnostics had been suppressed. The role is private; package and lock parse-result AST/diagnostic fields are crate-private with public read-only getters, preventing external mutation of parser provenance. The regression exercises duplicate `requires test` declarations. Both reviews found no remaining issues after the fixes. Six focused tests and all 973 workspace tests pass; warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that comparator order and partial-version precision are part of canonical requirement equality, that repeated target/`only` members are set duplicates rather than meaning changes, and that source/grant changes belong to the later Mode A checks rather than root-block comparison.

### IR-228: Select versions with deterministic MVS

- **Decision:** Add a pure MVS selector over preloaded, already filtered package contracts. Visit all named versions and the reached lock-floor version for each node unless a root path override pins that node. A `from path` pin discovered during traversal determines that node's final version but does not remove a floor contract already in, or later added to, the candidate closure; that contract may still name versions of other nodes. Keep root override pins distinct because they are known before traversal and suppress their node's floor. Reject ordinary edges that do not name their requirement minimum, require a `from path` version to satisfy its edge, and defer a root path override's range failure to the selected-graph check so it can become `VERSION_CONFLICT`.
- **Reason:** This implements resolver §2.4.1–2.4.2 without mixing source fetching or selected-graph validation into version selection. Queue and edge ordering are explicit, and contract, floor, and pin hash maps are lookup-only so input-map iteration cannot affect results. Reached-only validation avoids rejecting malformed data for unreachable lock nodes or contracts.
- **Review:** Hostile review identified and resolved the ambiguity between dynamically discovered path pins and queue-order-independent candidate closure. Follow-up review confirmed that the updated §2.4.5 distinguishes pinned and unpinned minimality, and that root path override range failures reach phase 2. A second independent review found no implementation defects and identified two documentation inaccuracies: the termination bound omitted reached floor versions, and the `selected` field comment omitted path pins. Both were corrected. Tests cover named-version maxima, unselected-version edges, reached-only floors, root and dynamic pins, conflicting pins, queue provenance, missing contracts, and cycles to the root; 16 focused MVS tests pass. The full workspace has 989 passing tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that floor contracts remain in the candidate closure when a dynamically reached `from path` pin selects another version, while a root `use path` override suppresses that floor from the start. This interpretation preserves closure independence from queue order but allows an unselected floor contract to affect another node.
- **Remaining scope:** Contract fetching, source choice and path inspection, selected-graph range/cycle checks, and user-facing conflict diagnostics are not implemented by this selector.

### IR-229: Build and validate the selected dependency graph

- **Decision:** Build `G` from root edges and the contracts of selected package versions only. Check every included edge against its selected version, sort conflicts by target node and requirer, and detect one deterministic dependency cycle with iterative DFS; rotate its package-version path to the least package version and preserve every cycle edge span. Exclude the root from the graph's dependency-entry set while retaining it as the traversal source.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.4.3–2.4.4 defines the build graph from selected versions, excludes edges from unselected candidates, reports requirement violations without backtracking, and requires deterministic cycle evidence. Capturing the full root contract and every dependency contract read during MVS in the private `MvsSelectionOutput` lets phase 2 reject a same-version root with different edges and prevents callers from supplying a different dependency catalog. Phase 2 consumes this immutable selection snapshot.
- **Review:** Two hostile reviews covered phase semantics, cycle traversal/canonicalization, determinism, and span mapping. One found a P2 reuse bug where equal root package/version values with different root edges could be mixed; selection now captures and compares the complete root contract, and a regression covers that case. The checker consumes contracts captured by MVS, so a caller cannot substitute another catalog. Follow-up review confirmed the fix and found no remaining issues. Tests cover unselected-version exclusion, requirement conflicts and ordering, root-path range failures, canonical cycles with edge spans, incomplete selections, and root-contract mismatch. All 996 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that preserving every contract read during MVS in the selection result is an acceptable memory cost and that the exact parsed root spans should be part of root-contract equality. If catalog size becomes material, consider an owning resolution context that holds the contracts once and carries both phases without cloning them.
- **Remaining scope:** Source fetching, override/source selection, graph-level duplicate-line and capability checks, lock generation, Mode A/Mode B selection, and user-facing diagnostics remain future resolver stages.

### IR-230: Find duplicate package lines in the selected graph

- **Decision:** Group the root and selected dependency versions by package identity and compatibility line. Emit one finding per identity present on multiple lines, with its selected package version for each line. Sort identities and lines deterministically. Ignore versions that appear only in the MVS candidate closure and are not reachable through selected graph edges.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.9 counts resolved nodes, includes the root's own identity, and excludes unselected candidates. Keeping this helper as structured finding data lets the caller apply the root lint policy (`warn` by default or `deny` for `duplicate_lines`) and attach the §2.13.1 explanation chain without coupling graph analysis to diagnostics.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. Tests cover root identity participation, multiple identities and lines, deterministic ordering, and a second line reached only from an unselected candidate. All 998 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that one lint finding per identity should list all selected versions by compatibility line, and that the root counts as a graph package for this check. Confirm the separate diagnostics layer will set the lint level from root policy and attach one explanation chain per line.
- **Remaining scope:** Applying the root `duplicate_lines` policy through `sai_diagnostics::LintPolicies` and rendering the user-facing lint remain unimplemented.

### IR-231: Explain MVS selections and graph errors

- **Decision:** Add deterministic breadth-first requirement chains over every contract visited by MVS, with edge requirements and byte spans. Root-reachable paths take precedence; candidate branches reachable only through a lock floor start at that floor. Record whether each link's requirer version is selected so diagnostics can mark unselected candidates. Explain a selected version by its least naming edge, path/root override pin, or lock floor. Validate conflict explanations against the captured edge, selected target, rejecting requirement, and root-reachable selected graph. Accept cycle explanations only for closed, simple cycles whose edges and endpoints belong to the captured selected graph.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.13.1–2.13.4 requires shortest provenance chains, deterministic ties, an explicit lock-entry origin, selected-version reasons, and the causing edge for cycles. MVS visits more candidate contracts than `G` includes, so explanation traversal must retain that candidate closure while conflict and cycle validation must use root-reachable selected contracts only.
- **Review:** Hostile reviews found that a forged conflict could pair unrelated edges, that links lacked a selected-version marker, and that conflicts and cycles could be forged from contracts outside the selected graph or from malformed cycle paths. The implementation validates those boundaries and includes regressions for each case. A final independent review found no remaining issues. All 13 focused explanation tests and all 1,011 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that requirement chains may traverse the full MVS visited closure, including unselected versions, while conflict/cycle membership is limited to root-reachable selected contracts. Confirm that a floor-only branch starts at its lock entry and that `requirer_selected` means the exact concrete version matches MVS's selected version for that node. Source ranges are retained as byte spans; mapping those to package files and rendering the resolver diagnostics remain later work.
- **Remaining scope:** Diagnostic rendering, help suggestions, cascade suppression, and duplicate-line lint-level policy remain unimplemented.

### IR-232: Attach chains to duplicate package lines

- **Decision:** Add an API that returns one shortest provenance chain for every compatibility line in a duplicate-package finding. Represent the root's own line with an empty chain from the root, and a selected version reached only through a lock floor with the lock-entry origin. Reject findings that omit, add, or misidentify any selected-graph line for the package.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.9 requires every line and one explanation chain per line, and §2.13.1 defines the shortest-path and lock-floor provenance rules. Public finding structs can be constructed independently of the graph helper, so the explanation API validates the complete selected-graph line set before rendering any chain.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that public input could omit one of three lines while still producing a partial explanation. Validation now compares against all selected-graph versions for the identity, and a three-line regression rejects a truncated finding. Final review found no remaining issues. All 16 focused explanation tests and all 1,014 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the root's own compatibility line should be represented by a zero-edge chain and that a floor-only line should name its lock entry as chain origin. Diagnostic severity and source rendering are resolved by later package-driver integration.
- **Remaining scope:** Extracting the root `duplicate_lines` policy and rendering `DUPLICATE_PACKAGE_LINE` remain unimplemented.

### IR-233: Resolve package lint policies

- **Decision:** Extract `ambient_imports`, `runtime_contracts`, and root-only `duplicate_lines` from syntax-valid package contracts into `sai_diagnostics::LintPolicies`, retaining each first policy item's source location. Keep package role solely on `PackageParseOutput` and derive semantic-validation scope from it, rather than accepting a second role in `PackageValidationContext`.
- **Reason:** The lint resolver already defines policy precedence and the dependency cap. Supplying exact manifest locations lets that resolver emit a policy-denied lint with the source of its rule. A single role source prevents root-only graph policy from leaking into dependencies when parse and semantic-validation roles disagree.
- **Review:** Hostile review found the duplicated role could make `duplicate_lines` apply to a dependency when callers passed inconsistent roles. Semantic validation now uses the parse result's role, and dependency test fixtures are parsed explicitly as dependencies. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining issues. The focused package validation tests and all 1,017 workspace tests pass; warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `PackageParseOutput` should be the single source of package role for both parsing and semantic validation, and that lint policy extraction is called only after semantic validation succeeds. Source locations use the parser's original byte spans and the caller-supplied `SourceRef`/`FileId` pair.
- **Remaining scope:** Constructing and rendering the `DUPLICATE_PACKAGE_LINE` diagnostic from selected graph findings and their requirement-chain explanations remains unimplemented.

### IR-234: Build source-mapped duplicate-line diagnostics

- **Decision:** Add a graph-level API that derives selected-graph duplicate findings from one `MvsSelectionOutput` and returns all unsuppressed `DUPLICATE_PACKAGE_LINE` diagnostics. Give the lint no primary span because a graph-wide finding has no single source location; report each compatibility line and its shortest explanation chain as child notes and structured `data`. Resolve default, root policy, CLI, manifest, and `--deny-warnings` levels through the shared lint resolver. Construct manifest locations only from syntax-valid `sai.package` files in one VFS workspace snapshot, verify the declared package identity/version and the package header, validate every referenced byte span against a parsed `requires` entry with the same target and version requirement, and use each file's document version in `SourceRef.snapshot`. Root test requirements are eligible; dependency test requirements are excluded.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.9 requires one finding per identity and one explanation per line; §2.13.1 requires chain locations to refer to the requiring package's manifest and marks unselected candidate versions. A source map supplied as unrelated path/span metadata could render a plausible but false location. Binding it to the parsed VFS snapshot and exact manifest requirement prevents that mismatch while preserving per-document versions for diagnostic snapshot validation.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found no non-test graph-diagnostic entry point and showed that a caller could pair an in-bounds span with unrelated source metadata. The graph API and snapshot-derived, parsed requirement map address those findings. A second hostile review found two further issues: the first implementation put a workspace ID in `SourceRef.snapshot`, which must be a document version, and it accepted non-manifest paths. The implementation now keeps the workspace snapshot ID private for cross-file consistency, uses `SnapshotFile.document_version()` in source refs, permits root workspace paths ending in `sai.package`, and requires dependencies at `dep:<identity>@<version>/sai.package`. Regression tests cover VFS snapshot validation, distinct document versions within one workspace snapshot, `null` CLI document versions, invalid manifest paths, forged in-bounds spans, package/version mismatches, dependency test requirements, and policy severity. The focused diagnostic tests and all 1,033 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the graph-level diagnostic should have no primary span and use the generic message “one package is present on multiple compatibility lines in the graph,” with package versions and chains in child notes and structured data. Confirm that source data records document versions rather than workspace IDs, omits session-local `FileId`, and uses a single workspace snapshot to validate all referenced files.
- **Remaining scope:** No package loader or command driver currently calls this API; `sai_driver` does not depend on `sai_package`, and the diagnostic sink/cascade pipeline is not connected to package resolution. Wire the API when that production path is implemented. The current crate-level API validates and builds diagnostics but does not make CLI builds emit them.

### IR-235: Resolve root manifest lint levels

- **Decision:** Add `resolve_manifest_lint_levels` to parse `sai.package` directly from a VFS workspace snapshot and return a deterministic code-to-`ManifestLintLevel` map for root packages. Verify the file ID, root manifest path, and snapshot package identity/version against the parsed header; derive each `SourceRef` and document version from the snapshot. Preserve the first same-level duplicate, omit unknown and hard diagnostic names after semantic validation has diagnosed them, and return no settings for dependencies.
- **Reason:** Package contract §1.13 makes `lints {}` root-only, requires invalid lint names to be diagnosed and ignored, and treats identical repetitions as a warning. Diagnostics §11.3.4 requires manifest levels to retain the exact source location, and §11.3.2 places these levels below source attributes and CLI flags. Parsing from the snapshot prevents callers from pairing manifest spans with a different file or revision.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that independently supplied parsed syntax, `SourceRef`, and `FileId` could produce a valid-looking location in another file. The API now accepts only a workspace snapshot and file ID, parses those bytes itself, verifies root path and package metadata, and derives the source reference from the snapshot. Regression tests cover wrong package metadata, non-manifest paths, missing file IDs, conflicting syntax, invalid lint names, root-only behavior, first duplicate source location, and resolution through `resolve_lint_occurrence`. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. All 69 tests matched by the package validation filter and all workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that root package manifest paths are represented in VFS snapshots as workspace-relative paths ending in `sai.package`, with snapshot package metadata in `identity@version` form. Confirm that invalid lint names remain the validator's diagnostic responsibility while the resolver omits them from effective settings.
- **Remaining scope:** No package loader or command driver consumes this map yet. Callers must first run package semantic validation and then pass the resolved map into lint occurrence resolution; CLI configuration, package collection, and diagnostic-pipeline integration remain open.

### IR-236: Apply manifest lint settings to MVS diagnostics

- **Decision:** Bind both package `policy {}` locations and root `lints {}` levels to each `MvsManifestLocation` as it is created from a workspace snapshot. Have duplicate-line diagnostics read these settings from the root manifest internally; callers now provide only CLI level and `--deny-warnings`. Verify the root manifest version against the selection in both the aggregate and single-diagnostic entry points. Make `resolve_lint_policies` derive source metadata from a workspace snapshot, matching the source-bound lint-level API.
- **Reason:** The diagnostic resolver defines the precedence of policy, attributes, CLI, manifest, and default levels, but caller-supplied policy/manifest values could disagree with the source map used for requirement chains. Deriving them from the same verified manifest prevents forged or stale settings and lets the graph diagnostic apply the documented precedence directly.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that a missing root manifest let CLI `allow` suppress the diagnostic before root policy could be loaded. The single-diagnostic path now requires a verified root manifest before resolving any lint level; regressions cover this case and missing dependency source locations separately. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. All 1,042 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that root policy and lint entries should be captured with the same snapshot-bound manifest locations used for MVS chain notes, and that the changed package lint resolver signatures are acceptable before the API stabilizes.
- **Remaining scope:** CLI parsing, diagnostic collection/cascade handling, and a production package loader are not present, so builds still do not emit these diagnostics through a user-facing command.

### IR-237: Diagnose MVS version conflicts and dependency cycles

- **Decision:** Build `VERSION_CONFLICT` from the selected graph and its validated shortest provenance chains. Use the rejecting `sai.package` requirement as the primary location; store both chains, their root or lock-floor origin, and every link's source identity, target, requirement, selected status, and source location in diagnostic data. Keep the related `requirement_chain` for the rejecting requirement; render the selected-version chain as source-mapped notes. Emit hard errors without automatic fixes. For an upper-bound conflict from a dependency, suggest updating and patching that requiring dependency; if a pin is below a requirement's minimum, suggest patching the selected target. Suggest lowering a root requirement only if an ordinary root requirement named the selected version and the conflict is an upper-bound rejection. For a selected-graph cycle, require the supplied cycle to equal the graph checker's deterministic cycle, list the closed node sequence, attach a source location to every cycle edge, and retain the shortest visited-graph provenance chain to the closing edge, whose origin may be the root or a lock-floor entry. For a selection-time edge back to the root's own line, close the cycle at the selected root version while preserving each edge's separately named version and selected target version. Independent branches with missing contracts do not suppress a known root cycle; its explanation uses only visited, source-backed edges.
- **Reason:** Resolver §§2.4.3–2.4.4 and 2.13 require explanations for both sides of a version conflict, useful non-mutating help, deterministic cycle paths, and source locations for their edges. Diagnostic chapter §11.8 caps chain length and expects relation-oriented steps, while the current `ChainStep` type does not carry package requirement metadata. Keeping complete edge records in `data` preserves that information; the related chain uses package node names and a final requirement step to avoid rendering the relation twice.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that the initial override suggestion targeted the selected package even when the requiring dependency needed a patch; `ChainStep.name` duplicated the `Requires` relation; and checking only the canonical selection reason could miss a direct root requirement that also named the selected version. The implementation now selects the patch target based on whether the selected version is above the requirement minimum, represents related steps as package nodes, and checks every ordinary root edge that named the selected version. A second hostile review found that root cycles could end at the edge's named version instead of the selected root version, selected targets could be confused with named candidate versions, and selected-cycle diagnostics discarded the closing edge's provenance chain. The diagnostics now close at the selected root, record both edge versions, and retain the visited-graph chain. A follow-up review confirmed the implementation and asked for the generic chain-origin rule to clarify floor-only branches; §2.13.1 now specifies root precedence with lock-floor origins when no root path reaches that exact version. Regressions cover the review findings and unrelated missing-contract branch behavior. All 225 `sai_package` tests and all 1,056 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm the primary location for a multi-edge selected cycle should be its first canonical edge and that the primary for a cycle-to-root should be the closing requirement. Confirm that a cycle targeting the root's compatibility line closes at the selected root version while preserving the requirement's named version, and that root-cycle data records selected targets even when traversal used an unselected candidate. Confirm a cycle chain can originate at the lock floor that introduced its selected version, and that independent contract-read failures do not hide already-known root cycles. Confirm the proposed `sai update` and override targets are useful when only package graph facts are available, with no tag lookup.
- **Remaining scope:** The project still lacks the production package loader, resolver command, diagnostic collection, and CLI renderer that would call these APIs.

### IR-238: Collect MVS graph diagnostics in the driver

- **Decision:** Add `sai_package` as a `sai_driver` dependency, matching the architecture's crate graph. Add `RootDatabase::mvs_graph_diagnostics` to bind the root manifest and every visited dependency contract to one `WorkspaceSnapshot`. Validate the root manifest's session `FileId` against its package key and `sai.package` path. Dependency candidate manifests use distinct snapshot-local IDs and are validated against their parsed headers and exact `dep:<identity>@<version>/sai.package` paths; they do not use `FileInterner` keys because those keys identify compatibility lines, not concrete candidates. Collect complete-selection conflicts, selected cycles, and duplicate-line lints, plus source-backed root cycles when selection is incomplete. Require the dependency manifest map to match all and only the contracts read by MVS; pass duplicate-line CLI level and `--deny-warnings` through to the existing lint resolver.
- **Reason:** Package diagnostic APIs previously had no driver entry point. The driver owns the VFS session and, per the architecture, depends on phase crates; validating file-interner keys and snapshot metadata prevents a caller from pairing a valid manifest location with another session's file. Unselected candidate manifests remain necessary for shortest explanation chains.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that same-line candidates collide in the stable `FileInterner` key, so unselected-version manifests could not receive distinct IDs. Dependency candidates now use distinct IDs within the workspace snapshot and are validated by package header, concrete-version metadata, and diagnostic path; a regression covers two same-line versions without interning either candidate. The root still uses and validates its stable session key. The review found no other issue in incomplete-selection handling or deterministic collection order. A follow-up noted that the first regression did not exercise a diagnostic chain through an unselected candidate; a second driver regression now verifies the candidate's `sai.package` path and document version in emitted chain data. Follow-up hostile review confirmed the fixture selects the intended version, the unselected candidate is not interned, and the mapped source is the shortest explanation; no actionable issues remain. All 1,060 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm the driver should require one distinct snapshot file for every MVS-read candidate contract, including unselected versions, while only requiring a RootDatabase file key for the root manifest. Confirm that only the graph diagnostics currently implemented in `sai_package` are returned here. Selection and fetch errors other than root cycles, package loading, path-source manifests, command-line collection, and human/JSON rendering remain separate work.
- **Remaining scope:** No package loader, dependency fetcher, resolver command, or end-to-end command diagnostic sink exists. This adapter returns source-bound diagnostics to the future pipeline but does not make a CLI command emit them.

### IR-239: Adapt validated manifests into MVS contracts

- **Decision:** Add `sai_package::resolver_contract` to turn syntax-valid, semantically valid root and dependency manifests into `MvsContract`s and resolver metadata. Bind root target filtering, root Git/path overrides, and root package identity to the exact root parse used to create the shared context. Preserve source, `only`, `grant`, test-edge, and source-span data for graph, lock, and diagnostic processing. Root test requirements participate in MVS; dependency test requirements do not. Direct path requirements and root path overrides receive concrete package versions from the caller, keyed by requiring package version and source spelling for direct edges, and by overridden identity for root overrides. No filesystem access or fetching occurs in this adapter.
- **Reason:** MVS needs a concrete named version for path edges, root targets to filter `only`, and root override information before selection. Keeping manifest validation and edge construction in `sai_package` avoids duplicating package semantics in the future loader and makes the boundary testable without I/O. Requiring the exact root parse prevents contexts from silently applying stale targets or overrides to a different manifest with the same package identity/version. Concrete path-package loading and source verification belong to the package-loading layer.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed source-override precedence, path identity and version checks, root-target filtering, root test edges, and omission of dependency test edges against the resolver specification. Regression tests exercise MVS selection for direct path dependencies and path overrides, including an override that must not pin another compatibility line. All 233 `sai_package` tests and all 1,068 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that binding a resolver context to an exact root parse is the right stale-input guard. Confirm that all root path overrides should be loaded eagerly to obtain their declared version, and that direct path manifests and their identities are validated by the caller before their versions enter this adapter. Confirm preservation of excluded `only` metadata and the choice to include root but omit dependency test edges. The adapter assumes caller-provided path packages correspond to the parsed source paths; filesystem resolution, source selection, and fetching remain follow-up work.
- **Remaining scope:** No production package loader, dependency fetcher, resolver command, or end-to-end diagnostic pipeline exists. The caller must resolve path sources and supply their concrete package versions; source choice, lock integration, and command-level diagnostics remain separate steps.

### IR-240: Snapshot contract requests at MVS dequeue

- **Decision:** Record every concrete package-version contract request in deterministic queue order, including requests whose contracts were not supplied. Each request captures the `named_by` edges known at dequeue time and whether that exact version is the node's lock floor. Preserve source clauses in the associated manifest resolver metadata; callers match them to request edges by requiring package version and span.
- **Reason:** Resolver §§2.4.2 and 2.8.2 choose a contract source when its candidate leaves the queue. The final `named_by` set can grow later as other contracts are read, so using that final set to select an earlier contract's source would change the specified deterministic decision. The lock floor is the source fallback only when no edge named that version.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed the snapshot is taken immediately after dequeue and before contract lookup or traversal of the candidate's edges, and that `named_by` plus `lock_floor` distinguishes edge-selected sources from a floor-only fallback. Regressions prove that a later requirer does not rewrite the earlier snapshot and that floor-only requests are marked. All 235 `sai_package` tests and all 1,070 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that manifest source clauses should remain in `ResolverContract` metadata and be joined to MVS request edges by requiring package version and span. Confirm that lock source is considered only for a floor-only candidate, when the request has no named edges.
- **Remaining scope:** This records source-selection inputs but does not choose a source, load a manifest, or fetch a package. A provider and production package-loading layer remain follow-up work.

### IR-241: Load MVS contracts from the priority queue

- **Decision:** Add `mvs::select_with_provider` alongside preloaded `mvs::select`. Request each concrete candidate contract exactly once when it leaves the deterministic queue, passing its sorted dequeue-time `named_by` snapshot and lock-floor information to the provider. Reject a returned contract whose package version differs from the request. Collect provider failures and continue independent branches; return the failures together with a boxed partial selection so callers retain known graph errors such as root cycles.
- **Reason:** MVS selection interleaves queue traversal with contract reads (§2.4.2), and source choice depends on the edges known when each candidate leaves the queue (§2.8.2). A provider callback makes that order explicit without preloading an unknown closure. Continuing after a failed read preserves independent selection diagnostics while preventing descendants of the failed contract from being invented.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed queue-order calls, the source snapshot timing and ordering, contract-key validation, and preservation of root-cycle errors on branches independent of a failed load. Tests cover deterministic transitive loading, dequeue-time source inputs, provider failures with a known root cycle, and mismatched provider results. All 238 `sai_package` tests and all 1,073 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a failed provider read should leave a `ContractNotProvided` entry in the partial selection while exposing the underlying provider error separately, and that independent MVS errors should still be reported. The provider is responsible for source integrity, exact package identity/version, parsing, and semantic validation before returning a contract.
- **Remaining scope:** This adds dynamic loading orchestration but no concrete filesystem or Git provider. Root lock floors and overrides still need source selection, fetched sources need divergent-source checks, and graph range checks, lock integration, diagnostics, and CLI wiring remain follow-up work.

### IR-242: Select sources for MVS contract requests

- **Decision:** Add a pure `resolver_source` adapter that chooses a request's source in resolver §2.8 order: a matching root Git override, a root path override for its pinned compatibility line, path pins, the source on the least-requirer named edge, then the lock source only for a floor-only request. Match each selected edge to validated requirement metadata by requiring package version and exact source span. Return every same-node path pin so the loader can compare normalized local paths and symlink targets. Bind every resolver contract to the shared root context; reject stale contexts and dependency-map keys that do not match the contract's package version.
- **Reason:** MVS source choice depends on the edges visible when a candidate leaves the queue (§2.8.2), and final source selection follows the same precedence (§2.8.1). Source metadata must stay joined to its validated manifest edge. Keeping filesystem, symlink, Git-tree, and URL operations outside this adapter makes its ordering deterministic and testable while retaining all evidence needed for path-pin conflict checks.
- **Review:** Initial hostile reviews found no source-precedence mismatch. A later pass found a P2 validation gap: a root override could return before checking all `named_by` edge metadata, and an unselected path pin with a mismatched flag could be missed. The selector now validates every named edge before applying precedence; regressions cover both cases, and the final hostile review confirmed the fix with no additional findings. Other guards reject dependency contracts built under a different root context or stored under another package-version key. Ten focused source-selection tests pass; all 248 `sai_package` tests and all 1,083 workspace tests pass. Workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that path pins should be returned as a full candidate set for the loader to compare after lexical normalization and local symlink resolution, and that root-context identity should be enforced for dependency contracts as well as the root contract. Lock source values are expected to have been validated and canonicalized by the caller.
- **Remaining scope:** No production filesystem/Git provider consumes this selector yet. The loader must resolve local paths and paths inside fetched Git trees, compare divergent path sources, canonicalize URLs, fetch and validate source contents, and compare the final selected source's contract with the contract read during MVS. Lock integration, package-loading diagnostics, and CLI wiring also remain open.

### IR-243: Drive MVS through source-aware contract loading

- **Decision:** Add `resolver_source_provider::select_with_source_provider`, which chooses the source from each dequeue-time `MvsContractRequest`, passes it and all path-pin candidates to a loader callback, and returns the MVS result, every successfully loaded dependency contract, and the source used for each successful read. Validate root and dependency roles, concrete package versions, and shared root-context identity. Preserve successful contracts and source records in partial results when a different provider request fails, matching MVS's independent-branch behavior.
- **Reason:** IR-240 records the queue snapshot, IR-241 calls a provider at that point, and IR-242 maps its provenance to a source. This adapter connects those stages so one source-aware provider has a stable API and retains the metadata required for later graph, lock, and diagnostic checks. The callback boundary leaves Git, filesystem, symlink, and transport behavior to the future loader.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable defects. It confirmed dequeue-time source choice, preservation of successful independent branches, package version/role/context checks, and delivery of all path-pin candidates to the loader. All 13 focused source-selection/provider tests pass; all 251 `sai_package` tests and all 1,086 workspace tests pass. Workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the loader callback should receive the full path-pin candidate set and must canonicalize and compare it before reading a contract; confirm that loader results must be dependency-role contracts built from the shared root context. Lock floors and lock sources are expected to have passed lock validation before being supplied.
- **Remaining scope:** No concrete filesystem or Git loader exists. Source fetching and integrity checks, path and symlink comparison, URL canonicalization, `DIVERGENT_SOURCES`, selected-graph range/cycle checks, lock writing, diagnostics, and CLI integration remain follow-up work.

### IR-244: Validate path-pin source agreement

- **Decision:** Add `validate_resolver_path_pins` to compare all candidate path pins through a caller-supplied canonicalizer. Reject a `PathPin` selection with no provenance or inconsistent source/reason metadata. On disagreement, retain both pin provenances and canonical keys; on resolution failure, retain the failing pin and callback error. Require a canonicalizer callback in `select_with_source_provider` and check pins known at dequeue before invoking the contract loader. After the queue drains, group every path-pin edge in the final `named_by` relation by node and compare the complete set, catching pins discovered after a version was read. The canonical key identifies a local path after lexical and symlink resolution, or a Git source by canonical repository URL, requiring package commit, and normalized repository tree path; Git paths that escape the repository must produce a canonicalization error.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.7.1 rule 5 compares joined local paths after lexical normalization and symlink resolution. Resolver §2.7.2 requires a path inside a Git package to be normalized in that requiring package's exact commit tree and rejected if it leaves the repository. The selector has no filesystem or Git access, so the canonicalizer remains loader-owned. Queue-time checks catch known conflicts before a load; the final check is required because a later contract can add an incoming pin after the target version has already left the queue.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that an empty pin list could bypass a `PathPin` consistency check and that the callback contract did not state that Git keys include the repository and commit or that repository escape must fail. The helper now rejects the empty inconsistent selection; API and implementation documentation specify complete source identity and the callback's escape check. Another review found that callers could omit the helper and load a selected path without comparing all pins. The provider API now requires a canonicalizer and rejects its failure before calling the loader; a regression proves the loader is not called and the failed request remains in the partial selection. A later review found that contracts loaded after a target version could add pins for that already-read version. The provider now checks every final named edge grouped by node; a regression constructs that queue order and verifies the late conflict rejects the result while preserving the partial graph. Review also found duplicate metadata errors between dequeue and final checks; matching reports with the same node and provenance are suppressed. The latest review found that suppressing by provenance alone could hide a later pin's independent canonicalization error and that a conflict pair could be reported twice in reverse order. The provider now caches successful keys and failed pin provenances, resolves each exact provenance at most once, still resolves newly discovered pins after earlier failures, and suppresses previously reported conflict pairs without regard to order. Regression tests cover each case, including preservation of later errors. The final hostile review found no new P0–P2 issues. The 23 focused source/provider tests, all 261 `sai_package` tests, and all 1,096 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The canonical key deliberately remains caller-defined so the loader can represent host filesystem identity and its Git backend's canonical repository/commit/path types without making this pure resolver adapter depend on either provider. The provider owns the mandatory dequeue-time and final comparison sequence; its caller remains responsible for returning complete keys and reporting Git repository escape as an error.
- **Remaining scope:** No production loader calls this API yet. Implement local and Git-tree canonicalizers, convert repository escape to `PATH_ESCAPES_REPOSITORY`, fetch and verify source contents, compare divergent sources and contracts, and integrate those results with lock generation and command diagnostics.

### IR-245: Compare resolver contract edges

- **Decision:** Add `compare_resolver_contract_edges` to produce deterministic, field-level edge snapshots for two dependency contracts of the same package version. Compare identity, parsed version requirement, named version, effective source after root overrides, `only`, and `grant`; ignore declaration order, spans, warnings, filtered edges, and dependency test edges. Canonicalize Git URLs and normalize `dir "."` to the repository root. Reject inconsistent contract/MVS metadata and incompatible root settings. Return `RequiresSourceBoundPathResolution` when matching edge fields contain path sources; only report `Equivalent` when every included edge is conclusive.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.8.3 requires the selected version's read contract and final-source contract to have equal included edges, while §2.5 defines target filtering and root override transformation. MVS does not retain `source`, `only`, or `grant`, so the adapter needs a stable edge representation beyond `MvsContract`. A path clause is relative to the source tree that supplied its requiring package; without that source identity, equal path spellings and versions cannot prove that the sibling source is the same.
- **Review:** Hostile review found raw source comparison would miss URL aliases and ignore root overrides; comparison now uses the canonical URL and effective overridden source. It also found that a dependency-relative path could be treated as equal across different source trees; the result now requires source-bound path resolution for every path edge, including root path overrides. A path spelling difference between two path clauses also remains unresolved rather than being reported different before the loader canonicalizes both. Git `dir` values are validated with `ContentPackagePath`, so `..` is rejected instead of normalized. The final hostile review found no new P0–P2 findings and confirmed these fixes. Tests cover all edge fields, URL canonicalization, root overrides, filtered and test edges, source-specific path-pin versions, root-context changes, unresolved path identities and spellings, and invalid Git source metadata. The 16 focused `resolver_contract` tests, all 269 `sai_package` tests, and all 1,104 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** `named_version` is included as a path-pin semantic even though §2.8.3's list names the written requirement; a source-bound path can resolve to a different declared sibling version, which changes MVS input. Path clauses retain their base package version and manifest spelling, but the comparator never treats them as fully equivalent until a loader resolves both against their actual source trees. Separate dependency contexts may carry different dependency path-package maps, while root manifest, targets, overrides, and root path-package versions must match. §2.8.3's unused-source review requirement conflicted with the fixed review schema in §4.7.1; IR-252 resolves that mismatch by adding the `Unused source clauses` heading and `unused_sources` JSON field.
- **Remaining scope:** Wire this comparator into a post-`Check` audit over selected graph dependencies; resolve final sources and reload only when source identities differ; provide source-bound local and Git path identities; compare all final-source contracts and report `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` with both source provenances and override help; define where unused source clauses appear in reviews; and connect the results to lock generation and diagnostics. No concrete source loader exists yet.

### IR-246: Compare source-bound path identities

- **Decision:** Extend resolver-contract edge comparisons with `compare_with_resolved_paths`. For every otherwise matching path edge, ask the caller to resolve its path against the source tree that supplied the expected or actual requiring package. Return `Equivalent` only when both sides resolve and produce the same complete source identity; return `Different` for unequal identities; retain the requiring package, edge package, path, and side when resolution fails.
- **Reason:** A manifest path is relative to the tree that supplied its requiring package. Identical spellings can point at different directories, while different spellings can identify the same directory after normalization and symlink resolution. The package crate has no filesystem or Git provider, so the caller supplies source-bound identity resolution while the comparison enforces the two-sided check.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found inaccurate path-test coverage and short-circuiting on the first mismatch or lookup failure. Tests now cover different spellings resolving to one identity, identical spellings resolving differently in the expected and actual trees, and multiple independent lookup failures after a known mismatch. The comparison collects every path-resolution failure and keeps its edge and side provenance; its ordered snapshots are private and exposed read-only. Final hostile re-review found no remaining P0–P2 findings.
- **Human review:** The caller-provided identity must include the complete resolved source: a local path after lexical and symlink resolution, or a Git source's canonical repository URL, requiring package commit, and normalized tree path. The `Expected` and `Actual` side must select the corresponding source tree for the requiring package. This API does not fetch final sources or decide which selected graph nodes need a reload.
- **Remaining scope:** Wire this comparison into a post-`Check` final-source audit; resolve final sources and reload selected versions only when identities differ; provide local and Git-tree canonicalizers and fetching; report `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` with both source provenances and override help; define where unused source clauses appear in reviews; and connect these results to lock generation and diagnostics.

### IR-247: Retain selected-source provenance

- **Decision:** Extend `ResolverSourceSelection` with structured provenance for the winning root path/Git override span, the selected path-pin edge, the least-requirer named edge, or the lock-floor package version. Keep all path-pin edges separately for consistency checking. Keep the selection fields private with read-only accessors, and validate that path-pin selections carry a matching winning pin and do not claim another selection reason.
- **Reason:** The final-source audit needs to explain both the source used during MVS loading and the source chosen after the full selected graph is known. A reason enum alone cannot identify the requirement edge or override that selected the source, which §2.8.3 requires in divergent-source diagnostics.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found public selection fields could pair an unrelated LockFloor provenance with another source. The selection fields are now private with read-only accessors, preventing callers from forging or mutating its winner metadata. Final hostile re-review found no remaining P0–P2 findings. Tests cover provenance for the least-requirer edge, both root override kinds, path pins, and lock-floor reads, and reject inconsistent path-pin origin metadata.
- **Human review:** The deterministic first sorted path pin is retained as the source-selection winner, while all pins remain available to prove they identify one resolved source. Override provenance uses the full override span; named-source provenance stores the MVS requirer and edge span; lock-floor provenance stores the exact package version.
- **Remaining scope:** Compute final source choices only after `Check`; compare source identities, reload selected contracts where they differ, use the contract comparator to decide equivalence, and build `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` diagnostics and review data.

### IR-248: Plan final sources for selected dependencies

- **Decision:** Add `plan_resolver_final_sources` to validate provider output against the completed MVS read set and plan source selections only for dependencies reachable through selected versions. Retain the queue-time request/source alongside a final request built from all accumulated named edges and its newly selected source. Recompute every read-time source in dequeue order using only previously read contracts and the same lock-source input, rejecting substituted provenance. Keep source-bearing contract requirements externally read-only and verify the remaining requirement metadata against MVS edges. Aggregate final source-selection failures per selected dependency. Keep range conflicts and dependency cycles in the graph, and retain selection-time edges back to the root separately so those known cycles do not stop planning.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.8.3 requires a post-`Check` comparison for each selected package version because later-read contracts can add a lexically earlier requiring edge and change its source. The selected graph excludes unselected candidate versions, while all successfully read candidate contracts remain available to validate the final request's edge provenance. This stage is pure planning: canonical identity checks and source reloads belong to the loader.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that root-cycle selection errors prevented graph construction, public requirement metadata could be changed independently from its MVS edges, and `sources_used` was trusted by package version alone. The planner now accepts only root-cycle errors as incomplete state, records those errors separately, and omits their root-target edges from the dependency graph. Requirement metadata is no longer externally mutable, and edge metadata is checked against MVS. Read-time sources are recomputed against the exact queue-time contract prefix and compared in full. Regressions cover late source changes, unselected candidates, both dependency and root cycles, independent final-source failures, substituted same-version source provenance, and changed lock-source inputs. Follow-up hostile review confirmed all three fixes and found no new P0–P2 issues. All 274 `sai_package` tests and workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Root cycles remain explicit selection findings because MVS does not select the root as a dependency node; they are retained beside the checked dependency graph while other MVS errors still block planning. Callers must pass the same validated lock-source map used during provider loading. The final request's lock-floor bit is true only when the selected concrete version equals the reached floor, and the full accumulated `named_by` set determines named-source precedence.
- **Remaining scope:** Resolve and compare read-time and final source identities, reload only when their identities differ, compare the reloaded contract edges with `compare_resolver_contract_edges` and its source-bound path resolver, then produce `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` diagnostics and review data.

### IR-249: Plan final-source reloads by identity

- **Decision:** Add `plan_resolver_source_reloads`, taking an immutable final-source plan, read-time source identities captured by the loader, and a final-source identity callback. Compare the captured identity of each selected dependency with its resolved final identity; return same-source and reload decisions separately and retain independent missing-identity or canonicalization failures. For a final path source, resolve its base first: use the final identity when `relative_to` is a selected dependency, and the captured identity when it is the root or an unselected package. Treat an unavailable or cyclic selected base as a failure rather than falling back to its read-time identity. Make final-source plan fields private and expose read-only accessors so callers cannot edit away entries or failures before identity planning.
- **Reason:** Re-resolving the read-time source after selection can observe a changed filesystem or Git reference and no longer describe the contract that MVS actually read. A source path belongs to the tree that supplied its requiring contract, including a contract from an unselected candidate, so the planner needs the source identity recorded at that actual read. A mutable public plan would let a caller silently omit a selected dependency from reload checking.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that the original callback could resolve both sides from current state without the actual read-time source context, and that public plan vectors could be cleared before planning reloads. After those fixes, review found that a Path from one selected dependency to another must use the requirer's final identity if that requirer itself changes source. The planner now resolves selected bases first, defers dependents until their bases are known, and reports unavailable or cyclic bases without falling back to a stale read identity. The next review found final-source selection failures were omitted from the identity audit result; those are now included alongside identity failures, and the affected node's read identity is still checked. Regression tests exercise direct Git identity equality/change/failure, missing read and base identities, a root-relative path, a selected path base whose final identity changes, cyclic selected path bases, and propagation of final-source selection failures. Final hostile review confirmed every selected node has either identity results or an explicit failure and found no remaining P0–P2 issues. All 277 `sai_package` tests and workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The loader must record a complete identity for the root and every successfully read package version, including unselected candidates whose contracts contribute edges. Local identity includes lexical normalization and symlink resolution; Git identity includes canonical repository URL, resolved commit, and normalized directory. A `Path` clause from a selected requiring package must be resolved against that package's final source identity; clauses from the root or an unselected package use the identity of the source that supplied that package's contract. Cyclic selected path bases cannot be assigned a complete identity by this planner and are reported as per-node failures for the loader to diagnose.
- **Remaining scope:** Compare reloaded contracts with `compare_resolver_contract_edges` and source-bound path resolution, report `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` with both source provenances and override help, define review output for unused source clauses, and connect the audit to lock generation and diagnostics.

### IR-250: Audit reloaded resolver contracts

- **Decision:** Add `audit_resolver_reloaded_contracts` to compare every selected dependency that the identity plan marks for reload. Keep the exact read-time contract in its immutable final-source plan entry; require reload results to pair each final contract and context with the identity of the source that produced it; and validate that the reload set and identity plan cover the same selected graph and source pairs. Compare dependency edges and resolve every matching path edge on both sides. Use the captured package identity for Expected paths and the selected package's final identity for Actual paths. For final-source path bases, derive root-versus-package ownership from source-selection provenance so a root and a dependency with the same package version remain distinct. Preserve per-side path lookup failures and source-selection provenance. Report the audit as equivalent only when it is complete and has no differing edge or path result.
- **Reason:** The IR-246 comparator needs source-bound resolution on both contracts, while the IR-249 identity plan is the authority for whether a selected dependency needs reloading and which final tree supplies its contract. Identity values supplied later in a separate map could describe another tree than the one that produced the read contract. Pairing each identity with its loaded contract and storing root identity separately preserves that provenance; pairing each reloaded contract with its final identity prevents results from another source being treated as equivalent.
- **Review:** Hostile reviews found that Expected paths could use a substituted identity map, unexpected reload output lost known source selections, read identities could be substituted before planning, and same-key reload contracts lacked source identity evidence. Later reviews found that lock-floor paths lost root ownership, identity plans could be mixed with a different final-source plan, and reload identity mismatch errors discarded both values. The provider now accepts root and dependency contracts paired with their loader identities, keeps its output read-only, binds each final-source plan and identity plan to the exact identified load, and rejects cross-load contract audits before any path lookup. Reloaded contract/context bundles must match the planned final identity, with expected and found identities retained on mismatch. Path-base provenance preserves root ownership for root overrides and root-relative lock paths, even when package versions collide. Unexpected reload failures retain available source selections. Tests cover read/final path bases, root and lock-floor path ownership, stale load and plan rejection, both-side path lookup failures, divergent identities, mismatched reload identity values, absent and extra reload output, and final-source planning failures. Final hostile re-review confirmed the fixes and found no remaining P0–P2 issues. All 279 `sai_package` tests and 1,114 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The package crate remains a pure audit layer. A filesystem/Git loader must return complete identities with the contracts it reads: local identities include lexical normalization and symlink resolution; Git identities include canonical repository URL, resolved commit, and normalized directory. Reload loaders must pair the parsed contract and final context with the identity of the final source actually read. The lock format defines path entries relative to the root directory, so a lock-floor path source must carry the root package as its base. These callbacks define the trust boundary; the package crate cannot independently prove that a loader's identity describes its bytes. A later integration step must translate incomplete or divergent results into `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` diagnostics, include both source-selection provenances and override help, and define where unused source clauses appear in review output.
- **Remaining scope:** Integrate final-source fetching and this audit into package loading; produce `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` diagnostics and review data; define review output for unused source clauses; and connect audit results to lock generation and command diagnostics.

### IR-251: Diagnose divergent resolver sources

- **Decision:** Add a `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` diagnostic builder for one reloaded-contract comparison and a helper that collects diagnostics from the completed audit. Report both source clauses and the manifest edges or root overrides that selected them, label the read-time and final selections as distinct resolved source trees, list both included-edge sets with their requirement, source, `only`, and `grant` values, and suggest making the root source choice explicit. Retain per-edge path-source differences as source-side path requests so the diagnostic can identify which relative path resolved differently. Bind each path comparison to its exact edge comparison with a lightweight shared token; before constructing a diagnostic, check that the reload, edge comparison, and path comparison refer to the same package version and comparison, and that the read/final source identities differ. Keep reloaded comparison internals crate-private so callers consume audit-produced comparisons without composing unrelated public fields. Capture requirement source clauses and root override metadata alongside manifest spans, and retain the root package identity with root override provenance so a supplied manifest must match the selection's root, span, and source.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.8.3 requires a divergent-source error to explain both source choices and the edges that selected them. Span-only mapping could otherwise point to a different requirement after a manifest edit, and a status value alone does not prove that the source trees differ. Path-only divergence also needs the exact differing edge, while canonical local identities may contain absolute paths forbidden by N-H-7.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that stale manifest text could be attributed to an old selection, a publicly composed comparison could report divergence without distinct identities, and path-only differences did not identify the affected edge. A second review found that path results could be paired with another same-version edge comparison and that root Git override spans lacked root identity. The diagnostic now matches named-edge source clauses and root override values against captured manifest metadata, preserves root package identity with override spans, rejects equal source identities and mismatched package/path comparisons, and uses a shared token to reject path results from another edge comparison. Path comparisons retain the package plus read/final relative path requests for each resolved identity mismatch. Comparison fields are read-only outside the package crate. Regression tests cover both source-selection locations and exact spans, stale same-length source-clause edits, changed root override source text, a same-span root Git override from another root, same-identity and wrong-package rejection, comparison-token separation, included-edge details, and path-difference details. Final hostile re-review confirmed both follow-up fixes and found no remaining issues. All 284 `sai_package` tests and 1,119 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Canonical loader identity strings are intentionally omitted from diagnostics because local identities may contain absolute host paths. Source clauses and selection provenance name the configured sources, and the diagnostic states that the resolved trees differ without printing canonical identity values. If two distinct Git commits came from the same configured URL, both displayed source clauses can therefore look identical. Lock-floor selections have no manifest edge, so their diagnostic cites the lock floor and uses the other source selection's edge when available. Confirm that this is the right privacy and provenance trade-off.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect this diagnostic builder to package loading and command diagnostics; implement concrete filesystem/Git source fetching and reload; define review output for unused source clauses; and connect the audit results to lock generation.

### IR-252: Preserve unused source clauses for package reviews

- **Decision:** Attach `unused_source_clauses` to each successful final-source plan entry. Collect clauses from incoming edges of the selected graph and from any other candidate edge that named the exact selected version, deduplicated by named version and edge provenance. Preserve the requiring package, named version, source clause, and source span. Omit the final winning named-requirement clause and all path-pin clauses checked as one final source-identity set. If a root override or lock-floor source wins, no requirement clause selected it, so retain every collected incoming clause.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.8.3 rule 3 requires package reviews to list source clauses that did not supply the resolved node. The final source planner already retains both the selected graph and the complete named-edge set, including edges from unselected candidates that named the selected version.
